# Cross-AI Plan Review — Phase 39

<!-- gsd:plan-revision-conflicts:begin -->
## Plan-Revision Conflicts
<!-- gsd:plan-revision-conflicts:end -->

> Note: one reviewer lane only (referee-one, owner instruction 2026-11-20); its findings are single-reviewer.

## Cycle 1 (referee-one)

### Review conditions (cycle 1)

- Reviewer: referee-one only (`.planning/config.json` `review.default_reviewers`), opencode `router-env/my-referee-one`, probed 00:21 READY.
- Six batches (A 39-01/03/06, B 39-02/05/17, C 39-04/08/09, D 39-07/10/11, E 39-12/13/14, F 39-15/16/18/19), run in parallel
  00:50-01:05, every lane exit 0 (prompts about 255 KB each: SPEC, CONTEXT, plan map, locked decisions, wave map, the batch's plans).
- Read-only review; the drivers are `tmp/p39-review/` (build_prompt.py, invoke.sh, run_all.sh, launch_one.sh), adapted from 38.1.
- Fix pass: each batch's planner verified every finding against the code, fixed, rejected with evidence or deferred, and wrote
  `tmp/p39-review/c1/dispositions-<batch>.md` (copied into the triage below); the orchestrator added cross-plan decisions.

### referee-one

#### Batch F (39-15, 39-16, 39-18, 39-19)

#### 39-15

**Summary.** The review-skill docs and contract tests are needed, but Task 1 stops before its first commit as written. The plan also removes the old caller input without wiring the new file list into the review workflow. That leaves the docs promising UI-lens selection and path validation that the documented command does not exercise.

**Strengths**
- The ordered comparisons against `ROLES`, `REFUSAL_REASONS`, and `LENS_NOTES` give the doc-contract test real sources of truth instead of three more hardcoded lists. The proposed eval cases also use the repository’s existing schema. [39-15 plan:180–200](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L180), [eval shape test:17–19](tests/test_skill_evals_shape.py#L17)
- Task 2 updates both initial dispatch and saved resume argv, and keeps the checklist’s empty availability map on the native route. Those are distinct consumers of the removed parent map. [39-15 plan:230–267](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L230), [existing resume contract:286–306](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-dispatch.md#L286)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Task 1’s generated-listing check always stops a clean execution.** The plan expects `gen_test_groups.py` to change neither file when it adds `test_lane_capability_docs.py`. Discovery does add the module automatically, but the generator then writes that module into the Makefile and hook listings. The status check tells the executor to stop, and the three-file commit cannot include the required changes. [39-15 plan:202–213](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L202), [generator:96–104](tools/gen_test_groups.py#L96), [discovery:296–317](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L296), [listing gate:570–583](tests/test_gate_scope.py#L570)
- **HIGH — The documented dispatch never supplies the values it says are checked.** Task 1 deletes `--tool-availability-json`; Task 2 deletes it from resume argv. Neither adds `--files`. The prompt separately interpolates document, grounding, and checklist paths, while 39-12 defaults `--files` to `[]` and validates the explicit list, skill directories, and MCP path—not those separately interpolated values or the tooling guidance paths. A document named in the prompt can therefore bypass the documented rejection, and a UI file named only in prose cannot select `ui_lens`. There is also a lower-level gap: checking the lens directory does not verify the resolved `SKILL.md` target before its contents are read. [39-15 plan:116–131](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L116), [reviewer prompt:14–24](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md#L14), [39-12 plan:149–154](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L149) and [204](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L204), [39-11 plan:139–140](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L139), [runner file resolution:6423–6438](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6423)
- **HIGH — Task 2 documents a protocol the Python reader would reject.** The planned runner emits `label` objects with `state` and `source`; 39-07’s `_well_formed` requires a string. Taken literally, every valid new preview becomes all-unknown in Python, including previews used to select a write-capable edit lane. The docs test checks words in a table, not this contract. [39-15 plan:245–257,269–273](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L245), [39-02 plan:143](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L143), [39-07 plan:131–133](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L131), [39-14 plan:119–125](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L119)
- **HIGH — The after-approval paragraph overstates the handoff.** It says the review waits for acceptance and never runs the pass unattended. The live `Approved` branch goes straight to Surface, and 39-14 supplies a separate command gated by the config value, not by an approval record or an interactive caller. D-14 leaves placement in the loop to Phase 40. Neither native built-in delivery nor the named skip on other routes has a caller in this plan; the native prompt update changes only tooling text. The default-off eval won’t catch either omission. [39-15 plan:149–177,198–200,267](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L149), [current Approved branch](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/SKILL.md#L382), [39-14 command:125–128](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L125), [D-14 handoff:200–209](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-CONTEXT.md#L200)
- **MEDIUM — The verification output cannot prove the stated test counts.** Both tasks ask for a number *passed*, and Task 2 requires zero skips. The bare parallel runner prints `Ran N` and a module status; skipped tests still count in `Ran N`. It only records skips when the report directory is set. Also, the R1 seed test uses `load_seed()` rows, not the JSON’s top-level `roles` array, so it does not provide the claimed single three-way lock. [39-15 plan:185–207,269–278](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L185), [runner reporting:480–510](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L480) and [936–943](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L936), [39-01’s seed-array assertion:156–164](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L156)
- **MEDIUM — The two executor tasks are not independently green or fully specified.** Task 1 ships a link to a heading created only by Task 2 and leaves the mandatory reference describing the removed input. Task 2 leaves the old `check_cbm_registered` explanation immediately before its replacement prose and misses the resumed exit-65 wording. Both tasks say to substitute flags if help differs despite the objective’s “copy exactly” rule. The objective names scratch HOME but supplies no command setup for direct helper calls; the all-files lint targets invoke `uv` without the provisioned/offline precondition. The proposed plan touches six tracked files before fixing generation; the new generated-file commit also triggers the full unit+arch hook, so its 1,800-second fit needs a budget rather than an assumption. [39-15 plan:83–87,108–113,131–132,202–203,230–241,277–286](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L83), [existing references:224–229,331–341](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-dispatch.md#L224), [Makefile:98–113](Makefile#L98), [shared hook inputs:225–230](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L225)

**Suggestions**
- Stage generated listings with the new test and explicitly run `tests.test_gate_scope`. Reuse the report-directory pattern from 39-12 for skip counts. Make the caller cutover coherent in one commit, or introduce tests and references in the order that keeps every committed workflow usable.
- Pin the actual protocol with one template-to-Python test, then document the observed fallback and label shape. Add exact fresh/resume argv for the validated file list, and use a UI-path fixture plus a rejected path fixture. Document the `context_form_applied` exception, the estimate’s exclusions, and “no lane spawned” rather than “nothing spawned”; `--print-argv` does start Node. [runner preview:296–318](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L296)
- Describe Phase 39’s edit command as an explicit owner-started operation with the required `--cwd`, approved `--files`, and separate accept/reject invocations. Leave automatic loop placement to the locked Phase 40 handoff.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** There is a deterministic execution stop, and passing the proposed text tests would still not establish the caller or wire contracts.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** R12 needs the tables, evals and test, not a new test mechanism. Reuse the existing generator and runner reports; compare the seed array in the same test rather than adding another weaker membership check. [generator:72–79](tools/gen_test_groups.py#L72), [runner reporting:480–489](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L480)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The fresh, native and resumed callers genuinely need updates. Replace the stale rationale instead of appending a competing one, and use the existing runner fixture for field shapes rather than maintaining another copy of every enum. [39-02 fixture contract:183](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L183), [existing reference:224–229](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-dispatch.md#L224)

#### 39-16

**Summary.** This plan puts the new settings where owners can find them and correctly separates advisory proposals from writes. Its main gaps are choosing the write destination before the wizard has resolved it, and treating a review-role preview as the capability of any selected lane.

**Strengths**
- The two eval scenarios exercise different decisions: no jev and a confident proposal the owner rejects. They extend the existing eval file rather than introducing a new harness. [39-16 plan:151–154](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L151), [existing config evals:194](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/evals/evals.json#L194)
- Task 2 gives exact anchors in the requirements and roadmap and leaves those files out of unit tests. The four fixed strings match what the closing plan looks for. [39-16 plan:175–198](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L175), [39-19 plan:96](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L96)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Task 1 can write to the wrong layer.** Step 1.6 invokes `confirm-skill-role` before Step 2.1 resolves and announces the destination. The example passes neither `--cwd` nor `--global`. With a global wizard invocation, it can create a project entry instead; when the wizard is operating on a different checkout, the command defaults to process cwd. A confirmed role is not consent to either destination. [39-16 plan:115–138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L115), [wizard target resolution:161–167](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/SKILL.md#L161), [39-08 command defaults:166–168](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L166), [local path helper:262–264](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py#L262)
- **HIGH — The displayed capability is for the review invocation, not necessarily the chosen lane.** The wizard tests every chosen lane through Step 2.5, but 39-13 passes literal `"review"` to the summary builder. An execute-only lane can be described as read-only based on its review argv. A user who skips the live usability probe also gets no pre-save capability from this step. The proposed text test does not inspect the capability instruction at all. [39-16 plan:140,145–150](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L140), [39-13 plan:145–151](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md#L145), [wizard-flow:46–48](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/references/wizard-flow.md#L46), [probe skip:248–271](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/SKILL.md#L248)
- **MEDIUM — The sorter’s published scope does not match its producer or cover both callers.** The prose promises *every* unsorted skill, but 39-08 filters out `addressable: false`. The config doc calls the confirmation CLI the only writer and describes MCP names in the same breath, without stating its name restriction or how an owner handles server names the CLI cannot accept. Also, D-09 names curated-config as a proposal-and-confirm caller through its loader; 39-09 supplies inventory, not that caller. [39-16 plan:126–142](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L126), [39-08 plan:129–133,166–168](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L129), [D-09:126–133](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-39-CONTEXT.md#L126), [39-09 handoff:208–213](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md#L208)
- **MEDIUM — Consent and “no proposal” are underspecified in the evals and checks.** Both evals start by asking for lane setup, but the optional sorter requires the owner to opt in first. The absent-jev eval expects a call without supplying that answer. Task 1 also repeats the unobservable “20 passed” check, and Task 2 spends the executor budget on four all-files lint gates for a two-file planning-doc commit without specifying offline setup. [39-16 plan:119–123,151–159,199–208](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L119), [runner output:936–943](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L936), [Makefile:98–113](Makefile#L98)
- **LOW — The roadmap note leaves the owner decision looking open.** REV-03’s new note says the default-off pass was confirmed, but the roadmap amendment leaves criterion 3’s “needs owner confirmation” unqualified. The four presence checks cannot catch that contradiction. [39-16 plan:180–187](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L180), [roadmap:975](.planning/ROADMAP.md#L975), [owner decision:52–54](.planning/docs/OWNER-DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md#L52)

**Suggestions**
- Keep classification read-only until the writer has an explicitly confirmed layer and project path. Pin the global, non-default-cwd, decline, and no-jev paths in the wizard contract.
- Display the preview for the actual role, with a dry-run path independent of usability-probe consent. Test one pair whose review and execute capabilities differ. Make the curated-config confirmation path explicit or surface the uncovered D-09 handoff rather than treating inventory as equivalent.
- Keep Task 2 mechanical: exact notes, one match each, correct owning sections, and an explicit supersession of the provisional decision. Put reusable scratch/offline setup and full help commands in the objective or task, not only in surrounding context.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The wrong config destination is a user-visible write error; the wrong-role summary undermines the purpose of showing capabilities before selection.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** The sorting step and summary are required. Reuse the existing destination decision, not a second implicit one, and extend the existing command-parsing test pattern to cover the documented flags and actual role. [wizard target:161–167](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/SKILL.md#L161), [parser test pattern:92–97](tests/test_spec_config_flow_text.py#L92)
- **LOW — Task 2:** Four formal amendment lines are enough. No new helper or repeated all-files lint is needed for this task if Task 1 owns the Python checks and the commit hooks remain enabled. [39-16 plan:179–208](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L179), [verification guidance](AGENTS.md#L280)

#### 39-18

**Summary.** The plan uses the right judge machinery and separates verdict evidence from post-judgment fixes. As written, however, it cannot safely begin under the no-live-call instruction, and its scoped gate can fail on valid historical records even when the current round passes.

**Strengths**
- It derives the candidate skill set from git, requires a frozen tree, and uses the repo’s same-rubric regression authority rather than accepting a score in isolation. [39-18 plan:113–134,145–151](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L113), [prior comparison:952–973](tools/judge_loop.py#L952), [bar calculation:1619–1636](tools/judge_loop.py#L1619)
- It keeps the useful two-referee coverage, nonempty-eval check, and no-rejudge fix record. The pre- and post-round coverage checks are doing different work: one checks pool size and count; the other checks for verdicts. [39-18 plan:119–120,145–150](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L119), [pool checks:30–65](tmp/judge-38.1/pool_check.py#L30), [eval gate:17–45](tmp/judge-38.1/evals_gate.py#L17)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Task 1’s ledger line does not authorize live calls.** It records the orchestrator’s choice of judges, not owner consent for probes and judge launches using the caller’s real HOME. The copied driver inherits that environment and starts the real CLI through the project runner. `AI_KIT_REFUSE_REAL_HOME=1`, the retry preflight, and an mtime comparison are not isolation or consent. The locked review instruction forbids live calls without a consent line; 39-19 also says no live call ran in the phase. [39-18 plan:79,105–121,185–188](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L79), [driver env and argv:47–48,132–143](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L132), [probe launch:337–356](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L337), [39-19 plan:24](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L24)
- **HIGH — Task 2’s current-round judge allowlist rejects historical verdicts.** The gate passes `--judges <this round's judge ids>`, but `judge_loop.py check` applies that list to **every round of every loop** for the selected skills. Both mandatory skills already have loop-32 verdicts from referee-three. A normal round with no substitution stops before fixes. The inherited gate instruction has the same problem; the actual prior-run `gate.sh` used a historical superset. [39-18 plan:149](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L149), [record validation:1298–1331](tools/judge_loop.py#L1298), [all-loop caller:1402–1419](tools/judge_loop.py#L1402), [review history verdict:46](.planning/judge/verdicts/32/ai-kit-spec-review/r1/opencode_router-env_my-referee-three.json#L46), [prior gate command:2](tmp/judge-38.1/gate.sh#L2)
- **HIGH — “All lanes ended” does not include the replacement process.** The plan backgrounds `lanes.py substitute`; the driver’s status only tracks assigned pool workers and native directories. `cmd_substitute` runs and ingests without registering in that accounting. The orchestrator can compare mtimes, release the freeze, or start publication before the replacement has finished. The post-round coverage check may catch a missing verdict, but it cannot restore a broken freeze. [39-18 plan:113,120–129,142](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L113), [status accounting:225–261](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L225), [substitute:447–459](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L447)
- **MEDIUM — The multi-skill round logic is inherited from a one-skill run without defining the mixed outcome.** The source instructions write one `s` into `skills.txt` for the next round. If both skills return `next-round`, following that literally can drop one. The copied `findings.py` also counts findings even after a valid refutation, so a refuted fourth finding can send a skill to an unnecessary round. Finally, the current plan calls a prior report row the baseline even though the tool uses the lowest valid verdict on the matching rubric pin: the review row shows 92.8, while its Phase 36 verdicts include 83.2. [39-18 plan:25–29,145–151](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L145), [inherited next-round instruction:167](.planning/phases/38.1-runner-error-classification-and-retry-policy-inserted/38.1-13-PLAN.md#L167), [raw count:23–39](tmp/judge-38.1/findings.py#L23), [report:29,56–57](.planning/judge/REJUDGE.md#L56), [bar calculation:1625–1636](tools/judge_loop.py#L1625)
- **MEDIUM — The final consistency check is after fixes, but the final dogfood check is not.** `dogfood.sh` runs before the records commit and any subsequent fix; `closing.sh` only checks judge records and notes staleness. That does not replace `check-dogfood` on the final tree. The previous closeout explicitly ran it after the gate. [39-18 plan:148–161](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L148), [closing helper:1–2](tmp/judge-38.1/closing.sh#L1), [dogfood checks:635–647](tools/check_dogfood.py#L635), [prior closeout:40](.planning/phases/38.1-runner-error-classification-and-retry-policy-inserted/38.1-14-SUMMARY.md#L40)

**Suggestions**
- Make explicit owner authorization a blocking precondition **before the first probe**; otherwise record JDG-04 as blocked. Distinguish that authorization from the unconsented D-18 capability spike, and from the judge-pair selection.
- Use the historical judge set for record validation and `pool_check.py` for current-round membership. Await a terminal result from every replacement before auditing, checking HEAD, or publishing. Include explicit behavior for an empty gated subset and for multiple skills advancing together.
- Use the tracked bar calculation for the baseline and a refutation-aware count for round decisions. Keep an unmet pre-fix verdict unmet in the summary unless the evidence establishes otherwise; a no-rejudge fix is not a new passing verdict. Carry the final dogfood result to 39-19.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The consent conflict blocks launch; the historical allowlist blocks an ordinary successful run; and the replacement path can weaken the evidence that the tree stayed frozen.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** Reuse `tools/judge_loop.py` and the existing lane driver. Do not add another launcher. Pin the exact scratch-driver inputs before starting; where the inherited scripts are unavailable, stop before any real call. [39-18 plan:99–121](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L99)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The required outputs are verdicts, the same-rubric bar, fixes, and verifier evidence. Avoid two competing regression calculations and use the report’s authoritative bar result. Apply the existing per-skill fix rule to the complete next-round set. [tools/judge_loop.py:1081–1098](tools/judge_loop.py#L1081), [AGENTS.md:65–79](AGENTS.md#L65)

#### 39-19

**Summary.** The closing plan sensibly separates an aggregate test run from metadata checks and preserves logs by commit. It needs a bounded failure path for the background run, a stronger same-candidate guarantee, and clearer limits on what the audit and scan can prove.

**Strengths**
- It reuses `make test` instead of reconstructing the tiers. The log parser checks positive totals, the installer result, the watchdog exit, and that the guard did not kill the run. [Makefile:59–89](Makefile#L59), [gate parser:25–45,67–84](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/check_gate.py#L25)
- Checking surviving processes before deleting the run’s temp directory is the right order. The config audit uses `stat`, not file contents, and does not treat permission errors as absence. [39-19 plan:126–139](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L126), [audit helper:11–23](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/real_config_audit.py#L11)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Task 2 can wait indefinitely, bypassing the “always run” cleanup.** `run_gate.py` writes the guard file only after the subprocess returns. Each `wait_gate.py` call waits for that file and exits 3 when it is absent; the plan repeats forever. An early wrapper failure before publication, or a suite hung without hitting the resource thresholds, never reaches cleanup. The “already ran” check also only sees a completed output file, not an in-progress run. [39-19 plan:124–126](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L124), [launcher:11–25](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/run_gate.py#L11), [waiter:10–20](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/wait_gate.py#L10), [watchdog:53–66](tmp/run-2026-11-19/orch-guard.py#L53)
- **HIGH — The final SHA can be green without final dogfood evidence.** Task 2 allows a repair commit and a new `make test`, but neither that aggregate target nor the closing plan checks that the project runner is still a pristine render and that the judge records reproduce the report on the repaired tree. Earlier dogfood evidence does not cover that SHA. The start/end git checks also do not pin the tree for the duration of the run; the waiter and checker independently read current HEAD. [39-19 plan:90,124–130,171–173](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L90), [Makefile:89,121–123](Makefile#L89), [waiter:10–12](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/wait_gate.py#L10), [checker:67–81](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/check_gate.py#L67)
- **MEDIUM — Task 1’s target scan does not prove the full “leave existing text alone” rule.** The inherited helper checks only added lines in a net diff. A deletion-only change to an existing banned-target test or doc passes; the self-check explicitly accepts that case. It also reports a path and excerpt, not a line number. This matters because the closeout’s prose implies broader coverage than the scan provides. [39-19 plan:70,97,101–111](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L70), [scan and self-check:21–55](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/added_line_scan.py#L21)
- **MEDIUM — Audit scope and failure handling need one rule each.** Task 1’s phase-start helper compares current mtimes with a commit timestamp; it is not a historical snapshot. The within-gate compare *does* snapshot and returns 1 on a change. The source plan treats phase-start changes as informational, while 39-19’s `must_haves` can be read to make all changes informational and Task 2 explicitly fails a within-gate change. Also, the phase-wide no-live-call statement must not erase 39-18’s proposed live judge activity. [39-19 plan:23–24,98–104,133–139](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L23), [phase timestamp check:17–44](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/phase_mtimes.py#L17), [snapshot/compare:26–42](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/real_config_audit.py#L26)
- **MEDIUM — “No surviving process” is stronger than the check.** The survivor helper matches checkout unittest argv or a run temp path in cwd/argv. A detached grandchild with neither marker is not counted, and a process whose `/proc` entries cannot be read is skipped. The watchdog can also retain raw argv samples on a kill; omitting them from the summary does not prevent writing them to the guard file. [39-19 plan:125–141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L125), [survivor matching:12–21,35–58](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/survivors.py#L12), [watchdog samples:55–65](tmp/run-2026-11-19/orch-guard.py#L55)

**Suggestions**
- Use one orchestrator-owned launch with a bounded deadline, an in-progress marker, and a terminal result on launch failure as well as suite exit. Record and verify the candidate SHA and clean-tree state throughout; always run the audit and survivor check before returning.
- Require final `check-dogfood` evidence for the same candidate, using only the permitted environment and no live spike. Report phase-start timestamp observations separately from the within-gate comparison. Report “no matching survivors detected” unless process tracking supports the stronger claim.
- Check zero, one, and multiple note matches explicitly. Keep the added-line scan for its stated purpose; separately check modifications and deletions to existing target content if claiming the full D-24 rule.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The ordinary green path is well specified, but a launcher failure can strand the orchestrator, and a repair can leave final runner-copy or judge-record checks unverified.

##### Ponytail findings
- **LOW — Task 1:** The note scan and metadata check are required and the existing helpers are enough. Create the destination directory explicitly before the first copy and keep the report to what those helpers measured. [39-19 plan:96–98](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L96), [metadata helper:17–44](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/phase_mtimes.py#L17)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Keep the existing aggregate and guard. The missing behavior is one reliable terminal result, not another layer of waiting or another suite. [Makefile:87–89](Makefile#L87), [launcher:11–25](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/run_gate.py#L11)

##### Cross-plan coverage and sequencing

- **Same-wave overlap:** None for the four reviewed plans; waves 9, 10, 12, and 13 each have one plan. Reuse of `tests/test_lane_capability_docs.py` by 39-15 and 39-16, and of `README.md` by 39-16 and 39-17, is ordered. **Missing dependency:** 39-16 needs the sorter commands from 39-08. Its chain through 39-15 → 39-14 → 39-13 → 39-12 → 39-11 reaches the shared scan but not the sorter. Fixed wave order masks this for a full run; it does not express the prerequisite for selective execution. 39-19’s 39-16 note dependency is transitive through 39-18 and 39-17. [39-16 plan:5–6,104](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L104), [39-08 plan:5–6](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L5), [39-17 plan:5–6](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md#L5)
- **Edge coverage is assigned more broadly than it is proved.** The R12 table-order, nonempty-eval and formatting edges have owners. R6 directory deduplication and ordering already have a shipped check. But R5’s read-once backstop is not met by one Python read *plus* runner preview and launch reads; the boundary test for the final budget also stops short of the composed block, whose omitted `SKILLS` lines are an explicit shortcut. R8’s missing-file-list edge is bypassed by defaulting the CLI input to `[]`. R2’s concurrency evidence is serial writes; the proposed read/splice/publish sequence can lose one of two overlapping confirmations even though each publish is atomic. [SPEC edge rows:536–540,553–575,590–594](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L536), [shipped R6 test:170–181](tests/test_runner_skills_argv.py#L170), [39-12 plan:193–201,204](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L193), [runner skill read:6485–6503](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6485), [39-08 serial test and writer:159–168](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L159)
- **Prohibitions have named owners, but three named checks are insufficient.** The R8 no-out-of-root rule lacks end-to-end coverage for caller-embedded and resolved skill-file paths, as noted under 39-15. The R9 no-unaccepted-edits rule is not proved by restoring only the listed files: the planned full-write lane is instructed not to touch other files, but the snapshot and diff only observe listed files. The final no-real-host rule is backed by the test runner’s environment and PATH tripwire, not by authorization for 39-18 or an offline guarantee for `uv`. [SPEC prohibitions:602–611](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L602), [39-14 plan:118–128,163–165](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L118), [test isolation:436–459](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L436), [Makefile uv invocations:64–78](Makefile#L64)

**Verdict: revise before execution.** Fix the deterministic 39-15 stop and runner/Python contract first, then resolve the config destination and judge authorization before any writes or live calls. This was a static, read-only review of all four manifest plans and their relevant handoffs; no tests, gates, probes, or live hosts were run.

#### Batch A (39-01, 39-03, 39-06)

#### 39-01

##### Summary

The plan gives REV-05 a clear foundation: one seed file, one closed role list, and one resolver that distinguishes classification from presence. It is not ready for unattended execution. Task 1 conflicts with 39-03 over generated files, and the downstream scan can turn missing or malformed seed data into a lane failure.

##### Strengths

- The resolver tests distinguish exact-case, kind-specific seed lookup from project-over-user role precedence. No seed row is treated as evidence that a skill is installed. The scan handoff explicitly tests absent seed skills and add/remove rescans. [39-01-PLAN.md:157–170](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L157), [39-04-PLAN.md:121–123](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L121)
- Task 3 reuses the existing layered config reader. The actual reader merges per key, project over user, and does not migrate or write either layer. [39-01-PLAN.md:227–240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L227), [`config_io.py:552–570`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py#L552)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — Task 1 collides with 39-03 Task 1 on both `Makefile` and `.pre-commit-config.yaml`.** The generator discovers tests from the working tree, not from each task’s staged files. In a shared checkout, either task can generate and stage a listing for the other task’s uncommitted test. In separate worktrees, both edits still have to merge into the same generated lines. Staging either file also invokes the full unit/arch path, contrary to running those jobs concurrently. Wave peer 39-02 has no declared file overlap. [39-01-PLAN.md:173–174](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L173), [39-03-PLAN.md:208–209](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L208), [`tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:227–230,296–298`](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L227), [`tools/gen_test_groups.py:96–104`](tools/gen_test_groups.py#L96)
- **HIGH — A seed failure can block a lane, despite the R1 prohibition.** Task 1’s `load_seed` raises on a missing or malformed file and only checks that `rows` is a list. A matching row missing `role` can raise from `resolve_role`. Task 2’s checker is not called by the planned scan; 39-04 calls `load_seed()` directly, and 39-12 calls that scan before composing a lane. The missing-module fallback in curated-config does not cover an installed module with a broken seed. [39-01-PLAN.md:169–170,205–208](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L169), [39-04-PLAN.md:135](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L135), [39-12-PLAN.md:149–150](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L149), [`gsd_catalog.py:28–42`](skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/gsd_catalog.py#L28)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2’s checker is narrower than advertised and can itself raise.** A list or object in `name` or `kind` makes the tuple used for duplicate detection unhashable, even after `_row_problems` records the bad shape. The two planted model cases do exercise real guard patterns, but those patterns only detect model literals in recognized positions or shapes; they do not prove that arbitrary seed fields contain no bare model or provider literal. [39-01-PLAN.md:200–208](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L200), [`tests/test_arch_hardcoded_models.py:48–64,96–123`](tests/test_arch_hardcoded_models.py#L48)
- **MEDIUM — The role contract is not fully pinned across readers, writer, and docs.** Task 3 accepts `unsorted`; 39-08 rejects it on confirm and 39-16 omits it from persisted values. The plan does not say whether a manually configured `unsorted` is a supported way to suppress a seed classification. Also, 39-15 compares docs to `ROLES` and checks membership of the seed rows separately; it never compares the JSON top-level `roles` list with both in the **one** test R1 requests. That test’s plan also tells the executor to stop when the generator changes the listings, which it will do for the new test module. [39-01-PLAN.md:158,234–239](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L158), [39-08-PLAN.md:162–168](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L162), [39-15-PLAN.md:185–202](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L185), [39-16-PLAN.md:142](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L142)
- **MEDIUM — Executor instructions disagree about formatting and isolation.** Task 1 requires one JSON row per line while the objective requires every written line under 100 columns. Task 2’s exact signature grep encourages another overlong line. Task 3’s bare `make` gates and all three tasks’ bare commits are outside the scratch wrapper; the gates and hooks invoke `uv run`, which does not enforce offline operation merely because the plan says to stop if it tries to download. The test subprocesses do get their own baseline, but that is not the environment of the outer commands. [39-01-PLAN.md:90,125,164,217,241–253](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L90), [`Makefile:98–113`](Makefile#L98), [`.pre-commit-config.yaml:20–43`](.pre-commit-config.yaml#L20), [`tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:436–460`](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L436)

##### Suggestions

- Have the orchestrator explicitly serialize the generated-file commits, or assign one owner to land both new test registrations together. Preserve the red/green evidence without bypassing hooks.
- Specify a visible degraded-seed path that does not block dispatch or invent presence. Keep the build-time seed tests strict. Put the scanner invocation in the test layer unless a production caller actually needs that callback.
- Pin persisted `unsorted` semantics and add one runnable code/JSON/SKILL.md comparison. Give the executor exact offline gate and hook invocation instructions and acceptance checks that work with wrapped syntax.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** The wave collision can stop the first commit, and the planned seed failure behavior conflicts with the non-blocking contract used by every downstream lane.

##### Ponytail findings

- **HIGH — Task 1:** The seed, role list, and resolver are required by R1/D-07/D-21. Package-relative lookup follows `tooling_guidance.py:22–40`; the existing generator is the right tool. The missing piece is one owner for generated state, not a second generator.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The seed check is required; a production scanner callback used only by tests is extra flexibility. Reuse the direct test-side `scan_text` precedent at `tests/test_cross_ai_docs_text.py:8`, and preserve the 39-15 docs lock.
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** The layered read is required by D-09 and correctly reuses `config_io.cfg_layered_table`. Keep it read-only; settle the persisted-role semantics with 39-08 and 39-16 instead of adding a second config reader or writer.

#### 39-03

##### Summary

The validator and shared Unicode fixture are appropriately small pieces of R8. The proposed tests establish the basic rules, but the dispatch handoff does not supply all the values the validator is meant to check. The rejection path also escapes characters without redacting secrets.

##### Strengths

- The eight fixtures distinguish raw control characters, malformed values, a symlink leaving the root, and a sibling sharing the repository’s name prefix. Whole-list equality also pins diagnostic order. [39-03-PLAN.md:190–191,202–206](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L190)
- The fixture generator lists the locked ranges independently of the implementation; Python checks the entire code-point space, and 39-05 consumes the same fixture in its sibling Node harness. No Python parser or new runtime package is needed on the Node side. [39-03-PLAN.md:127–157,232–234](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L127), [39-05-PLAN.md:134–146](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md#L134)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — The R8 handoff validates a list, not all the paths actually embedded.** 39-12 passes `files`, skill directories, and the MCP file. The shipped review prompt already embeds document, checklist, and grounding paths; 39-10 adds tooling paths during rendering. Those values do not reach `_rejections`. Worse, 39-12 prefixes relative strings with `cwd` before validation, which turns empty or whitespace-only entries into nonblank paths; a missing list fails in the caller before the validator can return its structured rejection. This leaves the R4 encoding edge and the R8 empty edge/prohibition materially uncovered at dispatch. [39-03-PLAN.md:202–206,237](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L202), [39-12-PLAN.md:151,201,204,240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L151), [39-10-PLAN.md:133](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L133), [`reviewer-prompts.md:18–30`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md#L18)
- **HIGH — Rejected values can still disclose secrets.** `_shown` escapes controls but returns the rest of the rejected path verbatim. 39-12 serializes that field to stderr, and 39-14 prints it for skipped paths. JSON escaping does not redact a credential carried in a path string. The package already has a shared redactor. [39-03-PLAN.md:200,205–206](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L200), [39-12-PLAN.md:201–204](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L201), [39-14-PLAN.md:155](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L155), [`secret_shapes.py:99–117`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/secret_shapes.py#L99)
- **MEDIUM — Path resolution loses distinctions the plan says it preserves.** A first missing component switches the entire path to non-strict resolution, which can suppress a later error rather than ignore **only** missing components. The direct loop fixture cannot catch that. Also, passing `{}` to `gsd_tools.project_root` bypasses its `RUNTIME_DIR` branch but does not clear inherited Git environment overrides in the subprocess; `-C` alone does not establish which checkout supplied the root. All planned root tests fake Git’s returned answer. The R8 encoding edge also has no filesystem fixture for a non-NFC name. [39-03-PLAN.md:201–206,235–240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L201), [`gsd_tools.py:34–48`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/gsd_tools.py#L34), [`tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:436–447`](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L436)
- **HIGH — Task 1 has the same generated-file collision with 39-01.** Both plans edit both listings and explicitly stage them. There is no declared overlap with 39-02. Task 2 is mechanically specified, but its bare generator and static gates do not follow the objective’s “every test or helper” wrapper rule; the defined wrapper only dispatches unittest modules. [39-03-PLAN.md:91,118–122,208–209,243–257](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L91), [39-01-PLAN.md:173–174](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L173)

##### Suggestions

- Give each embedded path a structured position and validate its original value before joining it to a base directory or rendering the prompt. Test an omitted list, blank list entry, rejected document path, and rejected guidance path through the actual dispatch seam. Keep the MCP run-directory exception local to that one field.
- Redact then escape **diagnostic copies**, never the original prompt or path. Pin the allowed roots and exact missing-component behavior at the helper boundary, including a non-NFC path and a clean lens directory whose `SKILL.md` resolves outside the roots. The planned reader currently resolves and opens that file separately from the directory check. [39-11-PLAN.md:139–140](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L139)
- Resolve the wave collision before dispatching Task 1. For Task 2, supply a separate, explicit invocation for the fixture generator and the offline gates rather than leaving the executor to adapt a unittest-only wrapper.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** The local fixture can pass while the shipping caller still bypasses the guard or emits a secret in a refusal record.

##### Ponytail findings

- **HIGH — Task 1:** A single validator is required by R8/D-13. Reuse `gsd_tools.project_root` for the probe and `secret_shapes.redact` for diagnostics, with the root-probe environment explicitly scoped. The small helper is justified; validating only an optional caller list is not a complete implementation.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The shared fixture is required by amended R6 and correctly reaches 39-05. The uncommitted generator is execution convenience, not a new maintained interface. Extend the existing fixture tests for filesystem behavior rather than creating a second validator or fixture framework.

#### 39-06

##### Summary

The window helper follows the locked precedence and uses the correct runner exit and describe fields. The main risk is its handoff: a helper given the right inputs behaves correctly, but the planned composer can supply the wrong context evidence, cannot pass a window record to the budget helper as written, and does not surface unknown windows.

##### Strengths

- The source confirms both the numeric rule and lookup pattern: `valid_tokens` excludes booleans and nonpositive values, `override_for` matches runtime and model exactly, and the existing catalog reader strips runtime suffixes and looks at the runtime row. Task 1 covers equal/over and UTF-8 byte counting without needing a model call. [39-06-PLAN.md:124–138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L124), [`catalog_context.py:9–10,68–72`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/catalog_context.py#L68), [`runner_dispatch.py:236–262`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L236)
- Task 3 reads the real exported shape rather than copying closed lists: `--describe` exposes the reason array, class keys, finding codes, and numeric exits. Exit 65 is absent from the status table. The Python reader uses the bounded process-group helper; the describe branch does not launch a host lane. [39-06-PLAN.md:187–196](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L187), [`cross-ai.mjs:1506–1529,6872–6883,6931–6933`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6872), [`runner_facts.py:30–44`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_facts.py#L30)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — The final-argv context exception is not established by Task 2 or its handoff.** The test injects `True` on pi, whose runner row has no context form. 39-12 derives the boolean solely from an `applied` setting report. The runner reports an existing Cursor context bracket as `ignored reason=id_carries` when asked to add another, and reports no context setting when none was requested. The final argument still carries the bracket; the Python lookup strips it and can cap against the base model’s window. The runner also appends `extraArgs` after building the named settings. [39-06-PLAN.md:162](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L162), [39-12-PLAN.md:194–195](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L194), [`cross-ai.mjs:1835,2027–2043,5359–5362`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L2027), [`detection.py:113–118`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/detection.py#L113)
- **HIGH — The R7 budget handoff is not executable as written.** `window_for` returns a dict, 39-11’s `plan_lenses` compares against `int | None`, and 39-12 passes the dict directly. With an eligible lens, the comparison raises, even when the record says the window is unknown. Separately, 39-12 sizes a draft block without SKILLS lines and explicitly allows a final refusal that could have been avoided by skipping one more lens. That leaves R5’s whole-lens budget rule and R7’s final-composition ordering short of acceptance. No planned block output carries the window’s `unknown` value or its `context_form_applied` note. [39-06-PLAN.md:135–138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L135), [39-11-PLAN.md:141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L141), [39-12-PLAN.md:194–202](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L194)
- **MEDIUM — Cache provenance is printed without validation or redaction.** Task 1 accepts any nonempty label and source strings, and copies them into the refusal detail. The repo already defines a closed label vocabulary. A malformed cache can therefore emit false provenance or expose text through stderr. This is separate from R7’s deliberate rule that an unlabeled positive integer can still cap a lane. [39-06-PLAN.md:136–138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L136), [39-12-PLAN.md:204,240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L204), [`catalog_labels.py:9–10,28–37`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/catalog_labels.py#L9)
- **MEDIUM — Task 3 can report green while the two contract checks were skipped.** Its `fails_when` requires a visible skip signal, but the wrapper uses the ordinary module runner. That runner prints only the summary for successful modules, and unittest skips still exit zero. The runner already has a JSON report path that records skips separately. Task 1 also requires a one-line signature match despite its width rule; Task 2’s conditional staging instruction disagrees with the objective’s blanket two-file staging command. [39-06-PLAN.md:86–88,149,170–171,192–202](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L86), [`tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:480–489,598–601,937–943`](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L480)
- **LOW — No same-wave write overlap, but a read/write race remains in a shared checkout.** The declared dependency on 39-03 correctly supplies both Python files. Neither wave peer 39-04 nor 39-05 edits them. However, 39-05’s final gate runs `tests.test_prompt_guard` while 39-06 is editing that module and its target. The gate should see either the committed 39-03 baseline or the committed 39-06 result, not an intermediate tree. [39-06-PLAN.md:5–9](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L5), [39-04-PLAN.md:5–11](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L5), [39-05-PLAN.md:152–165](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md#L152)

##### Suggestions

- Derive the context exception from evidence of the **effective** invocation, with the runner owning host flag semantics. Add an offline integration case for an already-carried context form, with and without a live override.
- Pass `window["value"]` to the budget function. Keep the full provenance record for display and refusal. Use one integer estimator for budgeting and final checks; test a boundary where the SKILLS section changes whether the last lens fits.
- Make the Node contract checks fail closed for the plan’s verification run, using the existing module report’s `skips` field or a required-Node preflight. Retain legitimate skips for ordinary no-Node environments.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH for integration; low for the isolated arithmetic.** A correct comparison function does not compensate for a wrong effective window, the wrong argument shape, or omitted user-visible evidence.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** The accessor and refusal record are required by R7/D-12. Reuse the existing `usability.find_runtime_key` at `usability.py:45–57` or the equivalent shared lookup rather than importing the private `runner_dispatch._row_matches` and writing another sorted traversal. Carry the result through 39-11 and 39-12 instead of duplicating the estimator.
- **HIGH — Task 2:** The edge tests are required and the scratch fixtures are sufficient for the numeric cases. The synthetic pi boolean is only a unit test; it does not exercise the actual final-argv handoff. Add that case at the existing preview/composer seam, not with a live-host spike.
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** The disjointness and exit locks are required. Reuse `runner_facts.read_runner_describe` and the existing skip-report path; no copied status table or separate harness is needed. 39-11 owns the lens-note comparison and 39-15 owns the docs lock.

#### Cross-plan coverage and release risk

**Verdict: revise before unattended execution.** The three plans fit the proposed task/file-count envelope on paper; I did not establish wall-clock fit. The immediate scheduling hazard is two generator commits triggering full unit/arch runs in the same wave. The high-priority product gaps are the R1 non-blocking seed contract, R8 validation at the real prompt boundary, and R7 use of effective invocation data.

Two additional handoff gaps affect the claimed phase coverage:

- **R2 concurrency:** 39-08 assigns the edge to a sequential two-write test. Its read–splice–publish sequence has no serialization across writers; atomic replace prevents a torn file, not the loss of one concurrent confirmation. [39-08-PLAN.md:159–166](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L159), [`toml_splice.py:780–792`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/toml_splice.py#L780)
- **R5 concurrency and no-silent-drop:** 39-11 claims one read per precheck, not one snapshot per dispatch. The shipped runner rereads the file from `--skill-path`, and 39-12 passes the path to both preview and launch. The plan does not establish that checked, budgeted, and delivered bytes match if the file changes during dispatch. [39-11-PLAN.md:30–32,139–140](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L30), [39-12-PLAN.md:198–203](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L198), [`cross-ai.mjs:6485–6503`](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6485)

**Review limits:** Read-only source review at `9e3f11e`. No files were edited, and no suites, make targets, or real host lanes were run. The other plans were checked as dependencies, wave peers, and handoffs—not independently approved.

#### Batch B (39-02, 39-05, 39-17)

#### 39-02

##### Summary

The plan puts capability detection in the right component: the runner that builds the lane’s argv. It preserves the existing stdout protocol and gives Python a separate stderr record. **Not ready to execute:** the planned producer and consumer disagree on the JSON schema, the first two task commits leave the `--describe` golden stale, and several parser rules turn incomplete evidence into definite capability claims.

##### Strengths

- The stderr insertion point preserves both existing contracts: stdout remains a three-field TSV line, and the final `AIKIT-XAI` status remains last. The existing Phase 37 render-sync test compares normalized stderr as well as stdout, so it will compare the new line without a second sync harness. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:7044–7065`; `tests/test_cross_ai_render_sync.py:77–102,260–276`.
- The plan correctly separates host-side skill loading from runner-inlined prose, and does not mistake Copilot’s built-in MCP disable flag for a ban on all MCP servers. It also keeps the new logic in plain Node and uses the sibling harness. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6521–6542,6887–6899`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/copilot/help.txt:121–123`.

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — 39-07 will reject every real capability record.** 39-02 emits a label object with `state` and `source`; 39-07’s `_well_formed` requires a string. The existing `print_argv_lines` forwards stderr as text without changing its JSON, so valid records will become `BAD_LINE_REASON` and all capabilities will read as unknown. That also breaks the write-capable-lane check in 39-14. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:139–144`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md:130–134`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:122`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:296–318`.
- **HIGH — Tasks 1 and 2 cannot both leave green commits as written.** Each adds row forms; `describeJson` copies them immediately, while Task 3 alone regenerates the byte-exact golden. The plan also lets Task 3 repair unspecified stderr consumers while permitting it to commit only two files. Move the golden update to each commit that changes the describe output, or land those changes together; name any actual consumer edits explicitly. This is separate from the accepted pre-push live-copy lag. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:135–136,161,176–182,230–243`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6853–6871`; `tests/test_runner_describe.py:407–419`.
- **HIGH — R3’s encoding and ordering edges are only partly implemented.** `flagValues` supports `=`, but `runAt` only matches literal token sequences. Thus a later `--sandbox=read-only` does not replace a previously recognized sandbox value. The tool parser always overlays list facts after mode facts, takes only one token from variadic lists, and treats any `*` in a list item as proof of shell access, even a pattern limited to another tool family. Recorded help documents repeatable, variadic, and pattern forms; it does not establish the proposed universal precedence. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:140–143,175`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:541–545`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/pi/help.txt:35–41`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/copilot/help.txt:97–116`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/claude/help.txt:81–84,256–258`.
- **HIGH — Cursor is advertised as writable beyond the evidence.** Task 2 emits `write: full` despite its own source saying write reach is unverified, and emits `shell: yes` from a flag that allows commands *unless explicitly denied*. A label does not prevent adoption of the value: the edit-pass selector checks only `write.value`. Likewise, the Codex conflict fixture assumes precedence between separate switches that the recorded help does not state. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:175,179–181`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:122`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/cursor/help.txt:26–48`; `tests/fixtures/cli_help/codex/exec-help.txt:53–63`.
- **MEDIUM — The tracer does not prove the dispatch contract.** The planted pi `--tools` override is a valid *preview inference* fixture, but actual review dispatch rejects it: pi’s review extra allowlist is `--skill`, and the check runs after the preview return. Meanwhile the plan adds the result only to the log branch, not to the shared planner result requested by R3. Keep the flip test, explicitly pin that dispatch still refuses it, and name the shared point where final argv and capabilities are returned. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:143–144,174`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1825–1834,2121–2127,7045–7066,7120–7134,7182–7187`.
- **MEDIUM — The argv allowance is a correct element limit but not a complete caller budget.** `102119` correctly subtracts the 281-byte reserve. The runner checks the *composed* prompt, though, including its preamble and any leading-dash newline; the planned caller budgets using a draft block without final skill lines. A boundary-sized prompt can therefore still reach the runner’s usage refusal instead of getting a whole-lens skip. The existing render-sync matrix also compares equality, not the presence or values of the line for an actual `--argv-file` invocation. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:143,175`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:194–201`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:3894–3909,7083–7089`; `tests/test_cross_ai_render_sync.py:66–73,260–276`.

##### Suggestions

- Freeze the wire schema in the task text for both plans. Preserve label provenance and pin one real-template-to-Python test before relying on synthetic strings. Cover no-model, unknown-runtime, and real `--argv-file` previews explicitly; distinguish unknown host capabilities from a known stdin transport.
- Use `unknown` for undecidable host syntax or flag conflicts. Test both precedence directions where evidence supports them, both Copilot shell names, and the real composed argv-prompt boundary. Do not change the review allowlist to make the preview test pass.
- Keep code, affected golden, and targeted tests green in each commit. No direct wave-1 file overlap with 39-01 or 39-03; serialize checkout-wide gates if they run in one checkout. Those two sibling plans **do** overlap with each other on `Makefile` and `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md:5–12`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:5–12`.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** Executed literally, the plan can stop at the first commit hook. Even if the commit order is repaired, the schema mismatch prevents the phase’s primary feature from reaching Python, and fixing only that mismatch exposes unsupported positive capability claims.

##### Ponytail findings

- **HIGH — Task 1:** Necessary for R3/D-02. Reuse the existing argv and report construction; don’t create a second parser in Python or a second copy of the prompt-limit calculation. The tracer needs its generated golden in the same commit. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:2132–2150,3905–3909,7043–7051`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Necessary to cover all hosts. The 18-row fixture alone takes about 300 lines in ordinary two-space formatting; with four row edits and the harness additions, this exceeds the review’s size heuristic. Keep one full matrix and one smaller consumer smoke test, using `runCaseProcess` rather than duplicating the matrix in both languages. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-selftest.mjs:2821–2838,3417–3439`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:173–207`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** The writer and stdout compatibility checks are needed, but the golden update cannot wait until this task. Reuse `_SkillCase._run` and the existing describe writer. The bare `node --check` and `make` commands do not inherit the separate scripts’ scratch environment; give the executor an explicit guarded invocation, with offline/no-sync dev tooling rather than “stop if a download starts.” `tests/test_runner_skills_argv.py:46–86`; `tests/test_runner_describe.py:413–420`; `Makefile:98–113`.

#### 39-05

##### Summary

This is a focused implementation of amended R6. The regex matches 39-03’s independently enumerated character ranges, the usage messages stay stable, and the resolved-directory test covers what raw-argument validation cannot see. The main gap is at the Python handoff: rejecting an unsafe lens in Node protects the runner, but does not by itself deliver the required per-lens skip.

##### Strengths

- Task 1 tests a real parse-time refusal, not merely a regex match. Task 2 tests a clean symlink argument whose resolved directory contains a rejected character. Both are meaningful against the current parse and load paths. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6485–6498,6694–6700,6921–6923`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md:107,139–145`.
- The fixture is independent of the proposed JS regex, and the test compares the entire rejected set. Existing skill tests already pin deduplication, ordering, and normal delivery; `--mcp-config` and `--argv-file` stay outside the new policy. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:126–157`; `tests/test_runner_skills_argv.py:89–105,170–181`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6694–6700`.

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — The resolved-path backstop is not fully carried into the lens caller.** 39-11 defines `check_lens` and maps rejected lenses to `path_rejected`, but 39-12’s wiring validates the row’s directory and passes `passing_rows` to the budget loop without specifying how `check_lens` produces those rows. The generic validator checks controls in the supplied string, not the resolved string. Unless the caller invokes the precheck explicitly, the same kind of lens that 39-05 correctly rejects can produce a runner usage exit instead of a named skip with the base lane intact. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:204–205`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:138–141`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:193–201`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6489–6498,6969–6972`.
- **MEDIUM — The diagnostic fixture checks policy scope, not safe display.** Task 1’s malformed MCP-path test only checks that the skill-path usage message is absent. The unreadable-file path includes the input filename, and the existing outer catch strips C0/C1 only. This leaves the review’s escaped-diagnostic requirement unproven. Keep the locked MCP acceptance policy unchanged; test safe rendering separately. The Python refusal handoff also escapes controls without redacting key-shaped values. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md:107,144`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6423–6427,6452–6458,6969–6972`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:200–205`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/secret_shapes.py:99`.
- **LOW — Make the task gates self-contained.** The objective requires scratch HOME for every helper/test, but both tasks call bare `node --check` and Task 2 calls bare `make` targets. The wrappers also resolve Node before setting scratch HOME. This is not evidence of a real-host call, but the commands do not enforce the stated isolation or offline policy. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md:74,110,117–119,147–165`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:130–133`; `tests/cross_ai_selftest.py:39–52`; `Makefile:98–113`.

##### Suggestions

- Keep the narrow Node change and the existing R6 regression tests. Add one dispatch-level fixture where the resolved lens directory is rejected, the lens gets `path_rejected`, and the base lane still runs.
- Make Task 2’s prerequisite explicit: Task 1 and 39-03’s fixture are present, and 39-02’s capability cases are green. No direct wave-2 overlap or undeclared dependency with 39-04 or 39-06. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md:5–9,100–114`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:5–10`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md:5–9`.

##### Risk Assessment

**MEDIUM.** The two-file Node task is small and mechanically specified. The downstream integration, not the regex, is where an optional lens can still fail an entire lane.

##### Ponytail findings

- **LOW — Task 1:** Required by amended R6/D-11. Reusing `parseArgs`, the existing usage message, and the sibling harness is simpler than adding a new validator layer. The multiple representative argument tests are useful; the fixture belongs in the full-set test, not runtime code. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6619–6631,6694–6700`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-selftest.mjs:2885–2904`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Required for the resolved-directory and shared-set checks. The explicit `--skill-name` regex check is redundant for current inputs, but it pins the requested common rule. Use the declared `[hex, name]` fixture shape directly instead of accepting alternate formats the producer never emits. Don’t cut the symlink test or the existing order/dedup checks. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-05-PLAN.md:139–146`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:157`; `tests/test_runner_skills_argv.py:170–181`.

#### 39-17

##### Summary

The release sequence is sound in outline: bump the shipped template and its pins, render through the installer, then measure the final shards. The repository contains the cited version pins and precedent helpers. **The live-write step needs revision before execution:** approval is delegated to an orchestrator rather than tied to an explicit owner decision on the dry result, and rollback can undo the very concurrent edit the installer preserved.

##### Strengths

- Task 1 keeps coupled pins in one commit and correctly distinguishes the shipped version from installer readiness. The latter still checks `_READY_TEMPLATE`, not the current generation. The existing golden writer also masks the inventory digest, so an added inventory field does not by itself force unrelated plan-golden churn. `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/cross_ai_script.py:314–318`; `tests/test_runner_describe.py:407–419`; `tests/test_curated_config_golden.py:210–229`.
- Task 2 reuses `ensure-runner`, compares dry and live bytes, and does not hand-edit the generated copy. Task 3’s JSON-report check catches a skipped shard that would otherwise look green in the module summary. The native checkpoint is appropriate for the known timing cost. `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/render.py:127–168`; `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/check_reports.py:14–32`; `.planning/phases/38.1-runner-error-classification-and-retry-policy-inserted/38.1-12-SUMMARY.md:45–76`.

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — Task 2 rollback can overwrite a concurrent owner edit.** When `write_script` detects that the target changed, it writes `.new` and leaves the live file alone. `render.py live` then fails its expected-result check. The plan says to preserve `.new`, but its generic pre-stage rollback restores the live file from `PRE`, erasing the edit that triggered `.new`. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:159–167`; `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/cross_ai_script.py:371–388`; `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/render.py:65–69,164–168`.
- **HIGH — Authorization and failure routing are not fully specified for the live write.** The ledger line is written by the orchestrator *before* the dry run; it is not bound to the checkout, original digest, dry result, or expected CONFIG migrations. The task prompt also excludes the `<orchestrator_steps>` block that requires dogfood and reversion before timing. A failed dogfood run may report an unrelated consent, flow, or judge-record issue, and reverting just the render leaves the template and live copy different. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:152,159–167,254–257`; `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/render.py:139–150`; `tools/check_dogfood.py:91–117,334–346,635–648`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 1 exceeds the executor’s file-count heuristic and has a preventable local prerequisite.** Fifteen paths are justified by the atomic version bump, but the task names the count rather than giving the exact staging command. The copied golden wrapper unconditionally requires `VOLTA_HOME`, even though the writer already uses `_real_node_executable`. The bare verify invocation isolates individual test modules but does not pin the parent runner’s TMPDIR to the plan directory. Neither the 1,800-second fit nor availability of ignored scratch helpers in the executing checkout is established by source inspection. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:119–137`; `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/golden.sh:3–11`; `tests/test_runner_describe.py:15–24`; `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:436–459,586`.
- **MEDIUM — The caller-facing contract and judge handoff disagree.** Task 1 raises the canonical cross-AI skill’s mandatory minimum, which changes what callers must do on template 11, but leaves both descriptions of `--print-argv` without the new capability line. 39-18 treats a number substitution as non-material; amended R12 specifically calls out rejudging `ai-kit-cross-ai` when its text changes. The handoff also needs to resolve the locked no-live-host instruction against 39-18’s explicit `start-probes`/lane steps; stale-digest notes are not judge evidence. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:133,254–258`; `skills/ai-kit-cross-ai/SKILL.md:59,85,229`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:457–462`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md:117–119`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md:21–24`.

##### Suggestions

- Make the dry preview, owner authorization, live render, and dogfood decision explicit orchestrator steps. Bind approval to the checkout and approved input/output digests. On `.new` or any unexpected live digest, stop and preserve both files; roll back only bytes the task can prove it published.
- Keep Task 1 atomic. Give the executor exact staging and offline verification commands, preflight the helper files where the task will run, and use the existing scratch/real-Node pattern. Move the task to the orchestrator if that preflight cannot establish enough time for checks and the hook.
- Make Task 3’s entry condition a named Task 2 commit **and** successful dogfood record. The report and log checks are good; use a fresh owned scratch directory instead of deleting a pre-existing reports directory. Keep the optional matrix remeasurement out unless separately authorized. Wave 11 has no peer; 39-16 is the declared predecessor and 39-18 the declared successor. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:5–6,189–218,254–257`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md:5–6`.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** Rendering through the installer is appropriate; the current rollback defeats its protection in the concurrent-edit case. The release also lacks an unambiguous authorization and judge handoff.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** Required by R12/D-23. The file count follows from real pins, not scope creep. Keep the existing golden writers and one buildable commit; finish the capability-line documentation instead of merely changing the minimum number. `tests/test_runner_describe.py:407–419`; `tests/test_curated_config_golden.py:210–229`; `tests/test_cross_ai_settings_text.py:183`.
- **HIGH — Task 2:** Required. Reuse `ensure_runner` and `check_runner_copy`; do not invent another publisher. The necessary addition is a precise approval and rollback rule, not a larger helper. `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/cross_ai_script.py:358–388`; `tools/check_dogfood.py:91–117`.
- **LOW — Task 3:** Required. Reuse `record_times`, the existing shard splitter, and the no-skip reports. The fixture and timing-table update are one logical commit; no new timing framework or speculative matrix refresh is needed. `tests/cross_ai_selftest.py:245–268,324–351`; `tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/check_reports.py:14–32`; `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:654–663`.

##### Coverage and verdict

**Request changes before execution.** All three manifest plans were reviewed. The scoped edge owners are present, but the R3 encoding/ordering and truthful-capability prohibition are not satisfied by the proposed parser. The R6 character set, ordering, deduplication and empty-value edges have concrete checks. At the downstream boundary, the R5 argv-size and read-once edges are claimed but not delivered: the caller budgets before the final block and the runner rereads the lens on launch. Likewise, 39-10 builds tooling-path lines while 39-12’s validator enumerates only files, skill directories, and the MCP file, leaving the R4/R8 embedded-guidance-path edge without an effective check. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:541–564,602–611`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:126–134`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:25–31,140–141`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:149–151,193–201`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6968–6972`.

Static review at `9e3f11e`. No files changed, no test suites or live hosts ran, and executor wall-clock fit remains unmeasured.

#### Batch C (39-04, 39-08, 39-09)

#### 39-04

**Summary.** The plan gives REV-05 a clear implementation path: scan disk on each call, resolve roles separately from presence, and expose a read-only JSON command. The main problems are at the boundaries: the scan does not retain all the roots the validator needs, and the MCP reader can report malformed or unreadable config as empty.

**Strengths**
- The CLI stays thin. Parser registration and command dispatch follow the existing pattern in `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py:80–102` and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1373–1398`.
- The add/remove tracer tests what R1 actually needs: presence changes on the next scan without invalidation, and a seed does not create presence. The MCP fixture also checks that a value nested under a server definition never enters the result. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:121–123,199–205`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — The root allowlist depends on whether a root happens to contain a skill.** Task 3 derives `skill_roots` solely from the parents of emitted skill rows. An existing empty skill root, or one containing only a guidance file, disappears from the allowlist. 39-12 passes that list directly to the R8 validator. Separately, the handoff checks the lens directory but not the resolved `SKILL.md` before reading; a contained directory is not proof that its file is contained. The existing curated scan explicitly checks both. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:205–210`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:149–151`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:140`, and `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/skill_scan.py:123–131`.
