- **MEDIUM — Task 3 conflates absent, malformed, and unreadable MCP config.** `data.get(key)` treats an explicit JSON `null` like an absent key, despite the plan saying a non-table is `unknown`. `os.path.exists` can also return false when a parent directory cannot be traversed, so the reader never gets the chance to report the error. The directory fixture exercises a failed `open`, not that case. The inherited cursor path also always uses `$HOME/.cursor` even when Task 1 honors `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` for skills; no fixture or source label resolves that asymmetry. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:130–132,200–208` and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/detection.py:1397–1435`.
- **MEDIUM — The names-only check does not make names safe to display or embed.** Task 3 accepts every string MCP key. JSON serialization escapes characters for transport but does not remove them from the decoded value, nor redact secret-shaped names. The downstream capability composer formats a `code_index` server name directly into prompt text. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:136,208–209`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:134`, and the existing redactor at `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/secret_shapes.py:94–103`.
- **MEDIUM — Verification can pass without running part of Config Doctor.** The Task 3 wrapper uses system `python3`, but `tests.test_config_doctor` is in the uv integration group and skips Textual cases when the dependency is missing. The checker accepts a nonzero test count with those skips; it only rejects the two inherited Node skip reasons. Its historical reference is also a bare filename rather than the phase-relative path. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:126,212–224`, `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:94–103`, and `tests/test_config_doctor.py:1589–1613`.
- **LOW — The tracer exposes a misleading intermediate CLI.** Task 1’s help advertises MCP inventory, but all hosts return `[]` until Task 3, including hosts whose state should be unknown. Give the intermediate command accurate help or land the help and MCP behavior together. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:135–139,208–209`.

**Suggestions**
- Record discovered roots independently of emitted rows. Pin an empty existing root, a symlinked skill directory, and an in-root directory whose `SKILL.md` resolves outside. Apply the file check before either the classifier or the lens reader opens it.
- Specify missing versus malformed MCP states, label config-path limitations, and make safe rendering a shared contract with the prompt consumer. Keep raw identity separate from display text.
- Use the repository’s uv interpreter for the Config Doctor verification and assert the intended cases ran.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** Every downstream lane trusts the scan’s root and name data. The ordinary fixture can pass while the boundary either rejects legitimate files or admits content from an unapproved location.

##### Ponytail findings
- **LOW — Task 1:** Needed for R1. Reuse the parser pattern and the fixture writer 39-01 already creates at `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md:238–240`. The CLI, scan tests, and 39-15 documentation handoff are named; no new abstraction is needed.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Plugin discovery and description reading are needed for R1/R2. Mirroring behavior rather than adding a forbidden reverse import is justified. Reuse the existing layered/project-scope fixture pattern in `tests/test_curated_config_skills.py:383` and assert order across two plugin roots, not just global before plugin. With ten fixtures and a copied parser/plugin reader, this is the plan’s largest task; reuse fixture setup and leave time for its commit hook.
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** Names-only MCP inventory and trustworthy roots are needed by R1/R8. Reusing the JSONC/TOML readers is lean; duplicating parser logic is not needed. Replace the declaration-count check for “unchanged” with a path diff, since the count at `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:227–228` cannot detect a changed body.

#### 39-08

**Summary.** The read-only proposal/write split is right, and the fake jev tests cover the normal advisory path. The plan is not ready to execute: the new writer bypasses existing project-config protection, and the classifier can consult jev under a different project from the one it scans.

**Strengths**
- The request uses the established choice protocol and puts description text in `context`, not `instructions`. The jev client explicitly treats context as untrusted evidence. The plan uses the existing fallback flags rather than inventing a second confidence threshold. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:119–131`, `skills/jev-fast-decision/scripts/jev_review.py:306–319`, and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/jev_names.py:121–137`.
- The planned writer uses the right splicer, not a serializer that would rewrite the whole config. `set_table` preserves unrelated blocks and verifies the result; `publish` preserves existing mode and uses `0600` for a new file. See `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/toml_splice.py:429–462,780–792`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — A project confirmation can write through a link into another layer.** Task 2 computes a project path and calls `publish` without the existing project-file confinement check. `publish` deliberately resolves symlinks and writes the target. This is precisely the case `cfg_confine_project_file` protects against in existing config workflows. The normal `--global` test does not exercise it. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:159–169`, `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:477–491,524–532`, and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/toml_splice.py:780–792`.
- **HIGH — Two overlapping confirmations can lose an accepted role.** The prescribed read–splice–publish sequence protects against partial writes, not stale reads. Both writers can read the same snapshot and the last replacement wins. The proposed test runs the commands sequentially, so it cannot establish the R2 concurrency claim. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:160–166`, `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cache.py:27–46`, and the repository’s lock-across-update precedent at `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/model_catalog.py:375–388`.
- **HIGH — `--cwd` scopes the scan but not jev’s settings.** The classifier uses `cwd` for discovery, then calls `ask_items` without carrying it to the child. `ask_items` supplies no subprocess `cwd`; jev chooses its project `[jev]` layer from `os.getcwd()`. A disabled target project can therefore be classified using the invoking project’s settings. The fake accepts arbitrary kwargs without checking them. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:119–132`, `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/jev_names.py:140–153`, and `skills/jev-fast-decision/scripts/jev_review.py:78–85`.
- **MEDIUM — R2’s “lists every unknown skill” path silently drops non-addressable rows.** 39-04 deliberately reports such rows as `unsorted`; Task 1 discards them before creating both the list and the request. Do not send unsafe names to jev, but list those rows safely with a reason they were not offered. The tests use only addressable names. Also, Task 3’s “failed” fake covers a timeout, not a non-zero exit with valid-looking stdout; `ask_items` currently parses the latter except for its special old-client case. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:129–132,190–196`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:224–237`, and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/jev_names.py:154–171`.
- **MEDIUM — The fake does not exercise the description trust boundary.** Task 1 sends printable text from any discovered skill path. The shipped ladder rejects recognized secret shapes before sending to a backend, but the test bypasses that guard and only proves that separately loaded MCP fields are not serialized. It does not cover a secret already in a description, or a linked file outside the scan roots. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:122,129–131` and `skills/jev-fast-decision/scripts/jev_ladder.py:186–195,244–262`.

**Suggestions**
- Reuse project confinement before reading or writing. Serialize the whole update per target, add a deterministic overlapping-writer test, and specify the exit and safe diagnostic for a publish `OSError`.
- Carry the selected project into the existing jev call without changing other callers’ defaults. Have the fake assert the child’s working directory and cover non-zero, malformed, disabled, and absent outcomes without calling a real client.
- Separate listing from eligibility. Validate the description file before reading it, bound the read rather than just the outbound text, and test that guarded data is not forwarded.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The write is atomic but can hit the wrong file or lose a confirmed role. The jev working-directory mismatch separately undermines project-specific settings.

##### Ponytail findings
- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** Needed for R2. Reuse `jev_names.find_jev`/`ask_items` and `tests/test_catalog_jev_names.py:110–139`; do not build a second ladder. Keep the classifier’s tests in `tests/test_skill_classify.py` as required for new modules, with test-list registration, rather than growing `tests/test_skill_table.py` as directed at `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:135`.
- **HIGH — Task 2:** Needed for owner-confirmed writes. `set_table` and `publish` are sufficient for editing the bytes, but not the whole write policy. Reuse the project guard and the locked-update pattern above. Add both tests at the command boundary, not just around `confirm_role`.
- **LOW — Task 3:** Needed for the empty, encoding and advisory edges. Reuse Task 1’s fixture and fake explicitly; substitute the failed-exit and non-addressable cases for more source-count checks. The final task is tests-only by its file list, but tells the executor to fix lint findings; specify how to handle a failure in Task 1 or 2 without violating staging scope (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md:183–201`).

#### 39-09

**Summary.** The plan correctly distinguishes GSD’s global-root mapping from the review skill’s host inventory. It preserves the five-key schema in the stated fixture, but that fixture does not prove the old behavior is preserved, and the fallback can either hide skills that are actually present or fail before producing inventory JSON.

**Strengths**
- Keeping GSD’s root mapping is necessary: gsd-core can report `.agents/skills` for Codex independently of `CODEX_HOME`. The frozen-output test runs against the old implementation before the change. See `.claude/gsd-core/bin/lib/runtime-homes.cjs:509–535`, `tests/test_curated_config_skills.py:465–488`, and `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:119–127`.
- The sibling loader preserves the cross-skill boundary, and an additive optional field need not change the old validator. Existing plan goldens mask the inventory digest, so adding one inventory field is not by itself a reason to regenerate them. See `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/gsd_catalog.py:28–42`, `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/plan_io.py:75–106,181–213`, and `tests/test_curated_config_golden.py:210–217`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — An optional role scan can now abort the existing inventory command.** Task 1 handles only a `None` loader result. A successfully imported module can raise: 39-01 explicitly prescribes `ConfigError` on invalid roles or TOML and `OSError`/`ValueError` for missing or malformed seed data. Those errors would cross `build_inventory` into a CLI that only handles its normal `(inv, error)` return. Task 2 mocks out the module rather than exercising either failure. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:129–130,154–155`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md:169,239`, and `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/cli.py:519–538`.
- **HIGH — Task 2 substitutes “no `skill_table` module” for the locked missing-package case.** Its mock leaves `gsd_tools` available. With the whole review sibling absent, `resolve_gsd_core` returns before `build_inventory` reaches the new fallback. The plan is candid about this limitation, but it is not acceptance of R10 as written. Even with only the module missing, the warning says the whole sibling is not installed and reports five empty lists despite installed skills. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:418–428`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:29–31,154–155`, `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/host.py:122–128`, and `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/gsd_catalog.py:23–25`.
- **MEDIUM — The frozen fixture cannot catch the family-filter change or prove the R10 adjacency edge end to end.** The prefix test admits names the old tuple excluded; the fixture plants none. It also checks the duplicate only in `ponytail_visibility`, while the new inventory test checks only a single Copilot skill. Finally, visibility rescans roots and reloads the seed instead of projecting one snapshot, so the two views can disagree if disk changes between reads. See `skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/skill_scan.py:22–29,293–306` and `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:119–129`. The GSD root mismatch justifies a different projection; it does not require rescanning the same root or changing family membership unnoticed.
- **MEDIUM — No curated-config owner-confirmation handoff is assigned.** D-09 explicitly names both config skills. 39-09 adds the inventory field only; 39-16 wires the optional sorter into spec-config only, and the curated `SKILL.md` edit in 39-17 is a version update. This is configuration-time sorting, not Phase 44 lens wiring. See `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-CONTEXT.md:126–133`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:58–62`, `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md:115–138`, and `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md:127–133`.

**Suggestions**
- Define the fallback for three distinct cases: module absent, module present but scan unavailable, and sibling absent. Use a named warning and exercise the full inventory validator, not only `_validate_ponytail_visibility`.
- Use the same run-local presence snapshot for both projections, adding GSD-only roots explicitly. Test a duplicate in both fields, a prefix lookalike under a global root, and a nonempty legacy visibility result in the fallback.
- Assign the D-09 curated-config proposal/confirmation step and its docs/evals explicitly. Include `tests.test_curated_config_golden` in Task 2’s named verification.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The compatibility tracer is useful, but it misses the new exception path and it cannot substantiate the locked missing-package promise.

##### Ponytail findings
- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** Needed for R10. Keep the loader and the existing inventory/fake-node pattern (`tests/test_curated_config_skills.py:95–139`). Do not reload the seed to calculate a presence-only projection or add another public scanner. Make steps 2 and 5 unambiguous: the frozen test is created once, run against the old signature, then updated in place (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md:127–131`).
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Needed for R10 degradation. Test the real loader failure mode and reuse `plan_io`’s full inventory validation rather than asserting just the shape of five empty lists (`skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/plan_io.py:75–106`). A separate ownership decision is needed for the whole-sibling case; a mock that leaves the required module installed is not evidence for it.

##### Cross-plan checks

- **Wave overlap:** No tracked-file collision or undeclared **same-wave** dependency among these plans. 39-04 is disjoint from 39-05 and 39-06; 39-08 and 39-09 are disjoint from each other and from 39-07. Their wrapper directories are distinct. One **later handoff** is missing from the dependency graph: 39-16 requires both 39-08 commands in its precondition, but its dependency chain through 39-15 leads back through 39-07, not 39-08. Strict wave barriers would mask this; a dependency-driven replay would not. See the plans’ frontmatter and `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md:5–6,104`.
- **Coverage:** Every R1, R2 and R10 edge in this batch has a named task or backstop. The substantive holes are R1 root ordering across multiple plugin roots and unreadable-config classification, R2 complete no-jev listing and concurrent confirmation, and R10 adjacency across both outputs and missing-package fallback. The R1 no-seed-presence prohibition has a scan test here and an unknown-runtime downstream check in `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:165–174`. The R2 data/confirmation prohibition is assigned but not fully tested as noted above. I did not independently sign off the rest of the phase’s edge matrix.
- **Unattended execution:** All three reviewed plans stay within the three-task and three-tracked-file limits as written. The four static targets exist, but the bare `make` commands conflict with the plans’ requirement to run helpers under scratch HOME. The wrapper runs only Python tests, while `make` uses `uv run`; “stop if it tries to download” does not prevent the first attempt. Put scratch HOME, offline enforcement, the exact per-task staging/commit instructions, and the summary destination in the task-visible prompt, rather than leaving the destination only in `<output>`. See `Makefile:98–113` and the wrapper/command instructions in `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:87,126,269–270`, `39-08-PLAN.md:84,125,258–259` and `39-09-PLAN.md:85,126,215–216` in the same phase directory. These plans do not edit the runner or add Node self-tests; I found no new model/provider literal or excluded-target work in their proposed changes.

##### Status: Issues Found — fix and re-invoke

Read-only source review at `9e3f11e`. No files changed, no tests or live probes run, and no claim that the hooks fit the 1,800-second cap.

#### Batch E (39-12, 39-13, 39-14)

#### 39-12

##### Summary

**Not ready to execute.** The shared composer is the right place for the dispatch checks, but the plan calls the wrong interfaces, leaves real prompt paths outside the validator, and explicitly allows optional lenses to cause a refusal that the base prompt would not have triggered. These are gaps in R4, R7, R8 and R11, not changes to the locked design.

##### Strengths

- The dedicated `LaneRefused` path preserves empty stdout and separates a Python-side refusal from runner failures. Exit 65 is free, and the proposed handler fits the existing dispatch error boundary. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:204`, `:240`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1298–1322`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1513–1529`.)
- The plan reuses the existing process-group-safe preview rather than adding another Node launcher. Moving route checks ahead of composition also avoids a preview for native and skipped review entries. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:142`, `:203`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:296–318`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/reviewer_dispatch.py:94–110`.)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — The tracer and lens wiring cannot work as written.** Task 1 calls `build_block` with seven arguments; 39-10 defines `build_block(BlockInput)`. Task 2 passes unchecked `lens_set` rows, which have no `bytes`, and a window **dict** where `plan_lenses` takes an integer or `None`. It also passes the scan name rather than `check_lens`’s `skill_name`; those can differ, and the runner can report an extra pathless skill. These are shape changes, not renames; the plan’s own stop instruction applies. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:105`, `:134–153`, `:195–201`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:127–139`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:137–141`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6496–6509`.)
- **HIGH — The fake runner output masks a real protocol mismatch.** 39-02 emits `label: {state, source}`. The 39-07 reader accepts only a string, and 39-12’s `PI_LINES` supplies strings. The tracer can pass while real previews degrade to all-unknown, losing the argv cap and the positive write facts needed by 39-14. Also, `or []` collapses a failed preview into “older runner copy,” contrary to 39-07’s distinct `None` and missing-line cases. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:139–144`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md:131–134`, `:159–162`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:153–157`, `:194–199`.)
- **HIGH — R8 does not reach the values production actually embeds.** `_rejections` sees only `files`, skill directories and the MCP file. Review prompts already carry checklist/profile, document, grounding and codebase paths before dispatch, and 39-15 removes the old tool-map flag without supplying those values. The builder independently emits tooling-reference paths; the lens precheck upstream resolves the directory but does not confine the resolved `SKILL.md` itself. Joining `""` or whitespace to `cwd` before validation also defeats the empty-value check, and omitted `--files` becomes an empty tuple. The rejection display escapes controls but does not redact secrets. These leave the R4 encoding edge, R8 empty/ordering edges and R8 prohibition unclosed at the dispatch boundary. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:151`, `:196–204`, `:240`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:199–206`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:133`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:139–140`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md:119`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md:17–30`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1347–1350`.)
- **HIGH — The final prompt is not what the budget loop measures.** The draft omits `SKILLS` and precedes the MCP-dependent block. The last check sums candidate sizes, not final applied forms, and never rechecks `prompt.max_bytes`. Near either boundary, the plan can refuse a lane instead of skipping a lens, or start Node only for the runner to return argv usage error. No output shows an unknown window or `context_form_applied`. Moreover, adding a block to an empty stdin prompt obscures the runner’s existing `empty_stdin` outcome. The tests cover a bare window boundary, not these composition boundaries. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:183–188`, `:194–202`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:322–329`, `:363–366`, `:565–569`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:7068–7088`.)
- **HIGH — Native and adapter callers are left behind.** Skipping native subprocess dispatch is correct, but no native caller receives the required lens lines. The scan returns disk-backed rows, `lens_set` selects only review/UI roles, and `simplify` is seeded as `edit_lens`; nothing connects the pathless built-in to its native Claude route or emits `built_in_no_file` elsewhere. Separately, the superpowers adapter calls `dispatch_execute` directly, supplies no file list, and does not catch `LaneRefused` at its CLI boundary. Keeping legacy fields avoids a constructor break but not the unhandled refusal. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:203`, `:237–240`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md:164`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:133`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:137`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/SKILL.md:280–289`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/dispatch_injection.py:384–397`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/cli.py:345–354`, `:490–495`.)
- **HIGH — Tasks 1–3 do not have a green, mechanical execution path.** Task 1 lists imports used only in Task 2, but Ruff runs at each commit. Task 3’s stop condition is met: 39-10 prints confinement only if the caller supplies text, and Task 3 supplies none. The execute-guidance test has no old fake guidance lambda to copy; the actual confinement assertions still require the target directory. An execute seam in `tests/test_ai_kit_spec_runner_dispatch.py` survives Task 2 and its file is not in Task 3’s edit list. Tasks 2 and 3 also span eight and six files respectively, before fixing these gaps, with partial CLI invocations and open-ended seam repairs left to the executor. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:103`, `:140`, `:174`, `:189–205`, `:224`, `:232–245`, `:258`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:127–131`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml:20–32`; `tests/test_ai_kit_spec.py:5063–5099`; `tests/test_ai_kit_spec_runner_dispatch.py:813–819`.)
- **MEDIUM — Preview count, cleanup and negative MCP evidence need explicit tests.** The unconditional two-preview design for lanes with additions is not reconciled with D-03’s once-per-key rule; each preview can wait 30 seconds. Cleanup only starts *after* directory creation, chmod and publication, so a publication error can leak the directory. The upstream block also still names a registered code-index server when the binary is absent via its “present, not enabled” loop; its absent-PATH fixture does not include a registration row. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:187`, `:194–202`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-CONTEXT.md:65–71`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:134`, `:167`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:296–315`.)

##### Suggestions

- Publish one exact integration contract before dispatching Task 1: wire format, `BlockInput`, checked lens row, integer window, preview failure representation, and required confinement text. Use a runner-produced capability line in at least one no-host test.
- Carry typed prompt inputs from the actual review and execute callers. Validate before reading or rendering them, and verify the final prompt and flags together. Pin a case where a lens fits the draft but not the final block, plus exact argv-cap, ignored-lens, and invalid-path cases. Protect MCP directory ownership from immediately after creation and test failure during publication.
- Give Tasks 2 and 3 explicit caller and test edits with bounded verification. Re-run the runner e2e module **after** switching execute, not only before it; its execute path exercises the real template with a fake host (`tests/test_cross_ai_runner_e2e.py:711–723`).

##### Risk assessment

**HIGH.** The literal tasks stop or fail before delivering the shared contract. Working around the stops would still leave path-validation and prompt-budget failures on normal dispatch paths.

##### Ponytail findings

- **Task 1 — MEDIUM:** Needed for R4/R11. Keep the tracer and the existing registration/test-group patterns; do not add Task 2 imports or duplicate 39-10’s carrier. (`skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py:89–102`; `tools/gen_test_groups.py:56–79`.)
- **Task 2 — HIGH:** Needed for R7/R8 and actual lens/MCP delivery. Reuse `check_lens`, `check_window` and `print_argv_lines` rather than adding a second path-only lens precheck or guessing return shapes. Require one caller-level test where real flags, checked inputs and final bytes agree. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:140–141`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md:138`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:296–318`.)
- **Task 3 — HIGH:** Needed for R4/R11. Build the actual execute spec once and reuse the existing target-specific confinement helper; do not leave the helper live only in a unit test or replace its behavioral tests with empty composer fakes. (`skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/execute_dispatch.py:39–49`, `:100–103`; `tests/test_ai_kit_spec.py:5042–5072`.)

#### 39-13

##### Summary

**Not ready to execute.** Adding a JSON key and appending status lines are sensible additive changes. The first task is explicitly blocked by the upstream summary format, and the proposed status and equality tests do not establish that the owner sees the capabilities of the lane that will actually run.

##### Strengths

- Keeps `check-reviewer` as one JSON object and preserves the three leading `config-status` lines. Those are real compatibility boundaries, including for the review chain’s first-line check. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:122`, `:150`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1347–1352`; `tests/test_spec_chain_text.py:114–129`.)
- Reuses `cfg_resolve(..., migrate=False)` rather than creating a second roster merge. The existing resolver reads pending rosters in memory and applies command-lane trust without migrating them. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:122`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:582–608`, `:626–639`.)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — Task 1 hits its own stop condition.** 39-13 assumes `summary_line` returns only the body; 39-10 specifies and tests the entire `capability …` line. Following the plan produces a double prefix or halts execution. The native fixture is also wrong: the reader normalizes a non-selector pinned model without a runtime to the session default, so the expected `native/fake-native` line cannot follow from that saved fixture. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:87`, `:114–122`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:122`, `:139`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:215–247`.)
- **HIGH — The summary previews a different lane.** Task 1 reads only nested `extra` from the saved row, dropping flat settings such as effort and escalation which the real reviewer path collects. It treats any runtime-bearing row as subprocess even when the current host can run it natively. Task 2 omits the actual `--effort` override and previews in `os.getcwd()`, not the scratch cwd used by the existing probe. Both summaries omit the final lens/MCP flags entirely. The spec-config handoff then promises that the text shows what the final argv allows. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:121`, `:150`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md:140`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/quota.py:42–52`, `:250–265`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/routing.py:168–183`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/effort_probe.py:58–87`.)
- **MEDIUM — The status command can emit misleading or partial output.** The plan prints the header before running a second config read and all lane previews. Its resolver and existing layer check can disagree: an empty project with `strategy = "local-only"` and a pending old roster already has a test expecting no lanes, but non-migrating `cfg_resolve` can read that legacy roster. A malformed hand-written lane can also fail during preview construction after stdout has been written. Runtime/model/key values go directly into plain-text output without control escaping or redaction; “plain text” alone does not guarantee one line per lane. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:121–123`, `:212–214`; `tests/test_config_status.py:177–186`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py:63–85`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:592–623`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/lane_settings.py:96–113`.)
- **MEDIUM — Task 3 verifies a happy-path fixture, not the R11 contract.** Both tests return fixed review-shaped facts from the fake and use empty scans, no context limit and no knobs. The execute case injects its own dispatch function, so it does not prove the default execute CLI uses the same seam. The standalone command also composes with an empty prompt and no knobs; once a lens fits the preview but not the dispatch, byte equality breaks. Task 2 specifies no seam changes for existing callers; for example the herdr-identity test fakes the effort preview, not the newly added capability preview. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:146–155`, `:169–181`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:154`; `tests/test_config_herdr_identity.py:94–112`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:163–182`, `:1256–1268`.)

##### Suggestions

- Pick one owner for the prefix and one source of effective lane inputs. Use the existing route and settings builders; distinguish an actual native lane from a configured runtime pair and label incomplete preview evidence as such.
- Test with a preview fake that derives its result from the received spec. Include flat settings, an effort override, a native selector, a delivered lens/MCP, and a lens at the dispatch budget boundary. Keep the R11 fixture comparison honest about which inputs are identical.
- Make status and summary agree on the same resolved roster, and build the response before writing stdout. Pin pending migration, local-only, untrusted command entries and malformed rows. Use the existing whole-tree read-only assertion, not just a single file mtime (`tests/test_config_status.py:138–161`).

##### Risk assessment

**HIGH for execution; medium blast radius.** Task 1 has a deterministic stop. If bypassed, the feature can tell the owner about a different lane from the one that will run and regress a command used to decide whether review setup is needed.

##### Ponytail findings

- **Task 1 — HIGH:** Needed for R11. A second formatter and independent lane normalization create drift; reuse the existing roster reader and 39-10 formatter with one explicit contract. The tracer lists six changed files including generated files, so name the narrow tests and expected output instead of leaving normalization to the executor. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:103–124`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:680–731`.)
- **Task 2 — MEDIUM:** The JSON field is needed. Thread effective probe inputs through the existing fake seam rather than add another default preview with a different cwd. (`skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:180–182`, `:1324–1351`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/effort_probe.py:75–88`.)
- **Task 3 — MEDIUM:** Parity is needed; a fixed `PI_LINES` response is too weak. Keep the existing CLI/spawn seam pattern, but assert the actual forwarded spec and final prefixed bytes. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:172–176`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:284–318`.)

#### 39-14

##### Summary

**Not ready to execute.** The default-off, separate accept/reject command is appropriate for R9, and preserving binary bytes is the right baseline. The proposed command is not yet a safe handoff to Phase 40: it can lose the rollback record, keep changes the owner did not see, and start a lane without checking the final write evidence or the approval state.

##### Strengths

- A literal boolean opt-in and separate owner decision fit the recorded decision. The fake test with non-UTF-8 bytes is valuable: it demonstrates why the saved content cannot be a text diff. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:112`, `:119`, `:158`; `.planning/docs/OWNER-DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md:52–54`.)
- Uses the supervised execute path rather than creating a new launcher. The runner does acquire a checkout lock before starting the execute lane; that is worth preserving. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:122–125`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:635–662`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:7161–7181`.)

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — Exit 75 can erase the only rollback record after a real edit.** Task 1 deletes the record for any `returncode == 75`. The runner uses 75 for `quota` and `transient` as well as `locked`, and those outcomes can follow a started lane. The fake tests only the true lock case. Delete a newly claimed record on confirmed no-start evidence, not on a shared exit number. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:125`, `:156`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1521–1523`, `:5899`, `:7163–7168`.)
- **HIGH — The runner lock does not protect the accept/reject transaction.** The plan checks for a record, snapshots files and publishes the record **before** the runner gets its lock. Two passes can both get past the existence test; the losing pass can replace or remove the winning pass’s record. A separate accept/reject can also act while the child is editing, or reject can overwrite a subsequent owner edit. A sequential “pending record” test is not proof of the R9 concurrency edge. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:121–128`, `:157–164`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1660–1662`, `:7161–7181`.)
- **HIGH — The owner gate and rollback scope are not enforced.** `[review]` comes from the ordinary project-over-user reader, not the trust-gated lane reader. With that bit set, the command accepts any `--files` list; no input binds it to final approval or an attended invocation. The instruction “change no other file” is not containment. Both `workspace` and `full` lanes can edit other files inside the workspace, while the summary and rollback see only recorded files. Deleted approved files can also break the post-run byte read before the owner sees a decision prompt. Phase 40 owns loop placement, but it needs a command contract that distinguishes approved, running, pending-decision and recovery states. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:54`, `:118–128`, `:163–164`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:552–570`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/execute_dispatch.py:39–48`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-CONTEXT.md:277–281`.)
- **HIGH — The edit lane bypasses the safeguards it depends on.** Task 1 repeats roster-to-spec and prompt assembly, omits `check_lens` and the budget guard, sends an empty lens report, and does not check the **final** preview’s write fact after adding skill flags. It previews under `req.env` but defaults to `run_runner(spec, prompt)` using ambient seams. The fake pi workspace capability is not a shipped fact: 39-02 expects pi exec `write=unknown`; it also proposes an explicitly unverified positive write fact for cursor, which this code would treat as sufficient. Tests need a faithful known-write case and a final failed/unknown preview that cannot spawn. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:112`, `:120–128`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:179–181`, `:198–204`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:139–141`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:284–293`, `:635–645`.)
- **HIGH — Tasks 1 and 2 disagree on safe restore, and Task 1 cannot be shipped independently.** Task 1 registers `--accept` before `accept` exists, calls the wrong `build_block` signature, and exposes `--reject` before record paths are revalidated. Run allows skill-root file targets, but reject enforces repo-only targets; it can skip restoration, delete the remaining recovery data and still return success. Containment alone also admits directories and missing paths under the 39-03 contract; byte snapshotting needs its own file-type and error contract. Task 3’s printed hint omits required `--cwd`. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:118`, `:125–132`, `:163–172`, `:186`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:202–206`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:127–138`.)

##### Suggestions

- Specify the transaction before handing it to the executor: atomically claim one pending pass, distinguish running from ready, preserve the record after possible edits or incomplete recovery, and refuse to overwrite an intervening owner change. Reuse `cache_write_bytes` for atomic publication, with explicit ownership and path checks around it (`skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cache.py:27–45`).
- Use a single approved-file policy at snapshot, diff and restore. Define behavior for missing, deleted, renamed and non-regular files and for changes outside the approved set. Require a file-scope enforcement or isolation strategy before promising that rejecting the pass removes all its changes.
- Hand 39-15 and Phase 40 copyable commands, approval preconditions, machine-readable outcomes, and the exact conditions under which review may finish. Test overlapping invocations, a real non-lock 75 fixture, and failed restore before exposing the CLI.

##### Risk assessment

**HIGH.** This is a local, single-owner tool, but one overlap, interrupted run, or misclassified exit can permanently lose the record needed to restore that owner’s files. The proposed tests do not cover those cases.

##### Ponytail findings

- **Task 1 — HIGH:** Needed for R9, but the tracer tries to design the whole transaction in one five-file task, including record schema, lane selection, prompt composition, diff and restore. It leaves helper boundaries and `EDIT_PROMPT.format(files=...)` to the lite executor and is likely to exceed the 300-line threshold once error handling is included. Reuse the shared composer and atomic byte writer; land a complete safe slice before parser registration. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:117–129`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cache.py:27–45`; `pyproject.toml:70–80`.)
- **Task 2 — HIGH:** Needed, but not as a later patch to an already exposed restore command. Specify malformed-record, incomplete-restore and actual concurrent-operation assertions rather than locking the test count at 12. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:151–175`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:7161–7171`.)
- **Task 3 — MEDIUM:** The CLI smoke test is needed. Exercise run, accept and reject with the same valid argv and scratch environment; capture the original seam before patching its module name so the factory cannot recurse. Test the command the owner is actually told to run. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:118`, `:128`, `:186–205`; `tests/test_config_status.py:44–49`.)

#### Cross-plan checks

- **Waves and dependencies:** Waves 6, 7 and 8 each contain only the corresponding reviewed plan. No same-wave file overlap or missing scheduling dependency in this batch: `39-11 → 39-12 → 39-13 → 39-14 → 39-15` is ordered. The failures are disagreements between the contracts, not missing dependency edges. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:5–6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:5–6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:5–6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md:5–6`.)
- **Coverage:** The affected SPEC rows have nominal owners, but the findings above leave R4 encoding, R5 boundary and read-once concurrency, R7 empty and final-check ordering, R8 empty and all-value validation, R9 ordering and concurrency, and R11 entry-point parity without effective end-to-end evidence. The prohibitions on unsupported capability claims, naming the code index without its binary, refusing instead of skipping an optional lens, embedding rejected values, and retaining unaccepted edits are not closed by the proposed tests. One additional unassigned acceptance check: **template-versus-rendered capability-line equality**. The existing render-sync test checks bytes and the rendered argv stdout prefix, not that stderr capability line. (`.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:253–256`, `:548–580`, `:586–589`, `:602–611`; `tests/test_cross_ai_render_sync.py:139–152`, `:174–196`.)
- **Verification and isolation:** The listed test-runner commands support explicit module names, and nonzero status or `FAILED` is a real failure signal. That does not make the test instructions green. The four Make targets exist, but call plain `uv run`; “stop if it tries to download” is not an offline guard. Set the offline and scratch environment **before** invoking those targets and commit hooks. No reviewed plan needs a new Node dependency, runner export, runtime shard, or live rerender. The proposed new model fixtures use the permitted invented forms; keep the existing hardcoding baseline and no-new-target rule intact when rewriting seam tests. (`tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:882–911`, `:937–943`; `Makefile:98–113`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml:20–44`; `tests/test_arch_hardcoded_models.py:265–286`.)

##### Status: Issues Found — fix and re-invoke

Read-only source review at `9e3f11e`. No files were changed; no tests, Make targets, or host CLIs were run.

#### Batch D (39-07, 39-10, 39-11)

#### 39-07

**Summary.** The plan puts skill and MCP flags in the right place and reuses the existing runner preview. But Task 1 rejects the capability payload that 39-02 actually specifies. Both real-runner tracer tests would fail as written, before this plan can hand usable facts to the block builder.

**Strengths**
- Appending the three fields and emitting flags in `runner_flags` preserves the existing path for ordinary calls and GSD hooks. Both builders already call that function, and the hook still checks every token before joining it. The tests pin flag order, unset-field compatibility, and rejection of paths with spaces. [39-07-PLAN.md:127–129](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L127) · `runner_dispatch.py:191–201,215–233,265–272`.
- The tracer uses the real template’s read-only preview without launching a host. It retains `run_in_own_group` and preserves the two-value `preview_setting` interface used by the effort probe. [39-07-PLAN.md:123–134](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L123) · `runner_dispatch.py:296–333` · `effort_probe.py:58–69` · `cross-ai.mjs:7016–7020,7032–7066`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Producer and parser disagree on label type.** Task 1 accepts only a string in `label`; 39-02 emits `{state, source}` for all four facts, including `unknown`. Thus a valid pi or Copilot preview takes `BAD_LINE_REASON` and fails the tracer’s `facts.reason is None` assertion. Task 2’s string-only fixtures hide the mismatch rather than protecting the wire contract. [39-07-PLAN.md:123–134,159–163](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L123) · [39-02-PLAN.md:139–144,173](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L139).
- **MEDIUM — The malformed-report guard is weaker than the task claims.** `_capabilities` accepts the first matching line even if a second contradicts it. `_setting_rows` accepts prefixes rather than complete records, and `form` accepts arbitrary lowercase strings. Meanwhile, `--print-argv` puts out exactly one capabilities line under 39-02 and has a defined settings grammar. Keep the old `preview_setting` API, but do not let a malformed or ambiguous record become an applied fact in the new parser. [39-07-PLAN.md:127,130–133,162–165](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L127) · [39-02-PLAN.md:144,173](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L144) · `cross-ai.mjs:2161–2163,7047–7056`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 1’s verification can miss skipped tracers.** The helper resolves `node` in `setUp` but does not specify storing it for `_run`; the executor has to infer `self.node`. More importantly, the specified command does not expose named skips: the test runner prints detailed output only on failure and records skips only when reporting is enabled. Task 2’s final gate can therefore report green without the two runtime-wire assertions executing. [39-07-PLAN.md:134–144,175–188](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L134) · `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:480–510,598–602,935–943`.
- **MEDIUM — An upstream intermediate-commit gate is not green as planned.** The required 39-02 tracer adds forms that `--describe` immediately publishes, but the golden is not regenerated until Task 3. `test_matches_golden` compares the whole object, so the first producer commit would already disagree with the committed golden. This is distinct from the accepted delay in re-rendering the live copy. [39-02-PLAN.md:135–161,239–240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L135) · `cross-ai.mjs:6854–6870` · `tests/test_runner_describe.py:407–410`.

**Suggestions**
- Pin the same structured label contract in 39-02, 39-07, and 39-10. Validate `state` and `source`, preserve both, and make malformed or duplicate capability records fall back to `unknown`. Use the exact producer shape in Python fixtures.
- Specify `self.node`, make required Node checks fail visibly or inspect the per-module skip report, and use fixed-string matching for the exact regex-source assertions. Move the producer’s golden refresh into each commit that changes `--describe`.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The argv change is small and compatible; the wire contract is a deterministic blocker. No same-wave file overlap or missing same-wave prerequisite was found with [39-08](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L5) or [39-09](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md#L5). Two tasks and four files are within the stated size limits on paper.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** Required by R4. Reusing `runner_flags` and `print_argv_lines` is lean; a second host-flag parser is unnecessary. The only new parser should consume the agreed wire format and hand it unchanged in meaning to 39-10. [39-07-PLAN.md:127–134](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L127) · `runner_dispatch.py:215–233,296–318`.
- **LOW — Task 2:** The edge tests are needed and should reuse the existing golden and hook tests. Comparing a spec’s defaults with the same explicit defaults adds little once both are checked against a fixed pre-change expectation. Keep that independent expectation and the unsafe-token case. [39-07-PLAN.md:166–174](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L166) · `tests/test_runner_flags.py:33–73,157–168`.

#### 39-10

**Summary.** The section layout and no-shell golden are useful, and `BlockInput` is a reasonable way to stay under the argument cap. The plan is not ready to execute: its downstream callers use a different signature, the old write-confinement warning gets disconnected, and some paths and claims can enter the block without the promised validation.

**Strengths**
- Reuses the tool inventory and the existing code-index evidence wording rather than rebuilding host detection in Python. It also avoids `shutil.which` falling back to the parent’s PATH when the child PATH is absent. [39-10-PLAN.md:125,132–134](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L125) · `detection.py:1351–1371,1495–1509` · `tooling_guidance.py:43–65`.
- The pi golden tests a strong negative case: even with executable fakes on PATH, a no-shell lane gets neither shell tooling nor the code-index CLI instruction. The separate registration fixture distinguishes an MCP registration from a live test. [39-10-PLAN.md:121,165–170](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L121) · `tooling_guidance.py:47–64`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — The handoff breaks review, execute, and the edit pass.** This plan defines `build_block(lane: BlockInput)`, but 39-12 and 39-14 call it with seven positional arguments. Those calls raise before they produce a block. Changing the calls mechanically is not enough: 39-12 removes the old confinement seam without supplying `soft_confinement`, so full/unknown-write execute lanes lose the warning. 39-13 also prefixes runtime/custom summaries that `summary_line` already prefixes. [39-10-PLAN.md:127,131,138–139](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L127) · [39-12-PLAN.md:152–153,197–200,237–239](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L152) · [39-13-PLAN.md:121–122](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md#L121) · [39-14-PLAN.md:125](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L125) · `execute_dispatch.py:39–49,93–97`.
- **HIGH — R8 does not reach the paths rendered here.** `build_block` resolves and interpolates both tooling paths internally. The resolver accepts any existing `AGENTS_TOOLING_PATH` override, while 39-12 validates only `files`, skill directory flags, and the MCP file. A tooling override outside every allowed root, or a path containing a control character, can therefore reach the prompt. The existing review caller also embeds document paths separately and is not told to forward them to the new validator. In the same output boundary, raw MCP names and lane identifiers are interpolated without the required display escaping or redaction; 39-04 accepts every string MCP key. [39-10-PLAN.md:129,133–137](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L129) · [39-12-PLAN.md:151,204,240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L151) · [39-04-PLAN.md:208–209](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L208) · `detection.py:1359–1371` · `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md:22,77`.
- **HIGH — Tool presence is overstated and sometimes printed despite the explicit prohibition.** `ENABLED_INDEX_LINE` tells the lane to use a fixed list of MCP tools even though `applied` confirms the config argument, not a connected server or the effect of its analysis profile. The presence-note loop can also print `codebase-memory-mcp` when the binary is absent: if the scan lists it as `code_index`, no route sets `line`, and the loop emits the name. The absent-binary test uses no such scan row. An ignored or unknown skill record with a `form=prose` or `name` can similarly be shown as delivered because those branches do not require `state == "applied"`. [39-10-PLAN.md:126,134–135,166–167](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L126) · `cross-ai.mjs:2133–2149,6468–6477` · [39-SPEC.md:605](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L605).
- **MEDIUM — Child PATH is not enough without child cwd.** For a relative PATH component such as `bin`, Python checks it relative to the dispatcher’s current directory; the runner starts with `cwd=spec.cwd`. The existing absolute-PATH fixtures cannot distinguish those directories. Also, 39-12 passes the Python child environment, not the final Node child environment after runtime overrides and `CONFIG.scrubEnv`. Shipped settings do not change PATH, but the plan does not prove the exact-environment contract for customized settings. [39-10-PLAN.md:127,132,168](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L127) · [39-12-PLAN.md:149–150](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L149) · `runner_dispatch.py:156–166,308–311` · `cross-ai.mjs:2695–2706,5351–5356,5692`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2 does not pin the full R4 acceptance surface.** There is no block fixture matrix for every runtime and role, and the test called `test_route_one_enabled_for_this_call` sets no competing registration, so it does not prove precedence. The golden also omits the host’s `skills` fact and its provenance from `ACCESS`; delivery rows alone cannot distinguish “no skill loading” from “unknown” when no lens was selected. [39-10-PLAN.md:121,130–139,165–176](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L121) · [39-SPEC.md:274–299](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L274).

**Suggestions**
- Propagate one `BlockInput` contract to every caller and choose one owner for summary prefixes. Preserve the existing scope instruction for full/unknown execute lanes, but do not say confinement is definitely absent when it is unknown.
- Resolve paths once against the effective child cwd/environment, validate them before formatting, and use existing `secret_shapes.redact` (`secret_shapes.py:99–116`) plus the R8 escape helper for diagnostics. Render only what the runner evidence proves.
- Extend the existing golden pattern using 39-02’s lane fixture. Include simultaneous MCP/CLI candidates, an absent binary with a registration row, malformed skill states, and a relative child PATH. Keep task-local commands explicit and make offline behavior enforceable before invoking the `uv run` gates.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The interface mismatch and unvalidated prompt values affect both dispatch paths. Wave 4 has no other plan, and the scan dependency is available transitively through 39-07. The two tasks and four files fit on paper, but Task 1 stages shared test machinery, which triggers the full unit/arch hook run. Reserve that time inside the 1,800-second cap rather than assuming a quick commit. [39-10-PLAN.md:89,141–152](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L89) · `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:227–230` · `tools/staged_mapper.py:261–283`.

##### Ponytail findings
- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** The renderer is needed by R4 and the summary by R11. Keep the input bundle; it avoids both the argument cap and an execute-module import cycle. Reuse `tooling_guidance.py:68–91` for the shared lines rather than retaining two implementations after migration. The work is incomplete until the dispatch and summary callers use the same interface. [39-10-PLAN.md:125–139](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L125) · `pyproject.toml:75–80`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The boundary tests are needed; a parameterized fixture is leaner and more useful than isolated tests that compare to the same formatter or never make two routes compete. Keep the independent no-shell golden. [39-10-PLAN.md:165–179](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L165) · `tests/test_runner_skills_argv.py:89–117`.

#### 39-11

**Summary.** The plan sensibly leaves lens delivery to the runner, separates selection from budgeting, and creates MCP files without copying host config values. The handoff does not yet wire those safeguards into dispatch. There are also gaps in file validation and final prompt accounting that can turn an optional lens into a whole-lane failure.

**Strengths**
- The tracer compares the Python byte count and digest with the runner’s report using multibyte content, and checks pi’s path form separately. The runner already has a fake-host test for exact prose body bytes, so Python does not need another inliner. [39-11-PLAN.md:132,138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L132) · `cross-ai.mjs:6520–6543` · `cross-ai-selftest.mjs:3155–3161`.
- `O_EXCL` and owner-only mode are appropriate for the run-scoped MCP file. The no-pick case correctly writes nothing: the runner rejects an empty `mcpServers` table. The plan tests both an applied and an ignored form without starting the server. [39-11-PLAN.md:167–172](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L167) · `cross-ai.mjs:6452–6477`.

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — The dispatcher does not call `check_lens` or carry its result.** 39-12 defines `rows` and `rejected`, then uses an undefined `passing_rows`. It never calls `check_lens` to populate `note`, `bytes`, or `skill_name`; it builds flags with the scan `name` rather than the checked runner name. 39-10 then matches reports by scan name. A fixture whose valid frontmatter name differs from its directory name is enough to expose the mismatch. Two different directories can also resolve to the same runner name; `loadSkills` pairs names with the first matching entry, and the renderer takes the first report, so that case needs a deterministic rule. [39-11-PLAN.md:139–141,197](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L139) · [39-12-PLAN.md:196–201](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L196) · [39-10-PLAN.md:135](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L135) · `cross-ai.mjs:6495–6511`.
- **HIGH — The validated directory is not necessarily where the body comes from.** `_read_body` follows `SKILL.md` itself with `realpath`, but only the containing directory was checked against allowed roots. An in-root skill can therefore read and inline an out-of-root file through a symlink. The proposed `isfile` followed by blocking `open` also loses the runner’s protection against a file changing type between check and open. Finally, the raw delimiter contract is already recorded as a gap: a quote in a path or a closing tag in the body can invalidate the claimed framing, and Task 3 pins that exact formatter. [39-11-PLAN.md:138–140,201](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L138) · `cross-ai.mjs:6423–6448,6492–6499,6520` · [39-SPEC.md:118–132](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L118).
- **HIGH — Budget tests do not cover the final prompt.** 39-12 budgets from a block without `SKILLS` lines or final MCP prose. It then checks only the context window, not the final argv byte cap. Near that cap, an optional lens can still cause runner usage exit 2; near the context limit, the composer can refuse instead of skipping the lens. The synthetic equality test cannot catch either. The read-once backstop is also not met: Python pre-check, final preview, and launch each read the source, so a file change can invalidate the size and digest before dispatch. [39-11-PLAN.md:31,139–141,198](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L31) · [39-12-PLAN.md:193–202](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L193) · `cross-ai.mjs:3905–3910,6969–6972,7083–7088`.
- **HIGH — The native-only `simplify` requirement has no producer.** 39-04 emits only rows backed by a `SKILL.md`; 39-11 selects only `review_lens` and conditional `ui_lens`; the seed assigns `simplify` to `edit_lens`. The pathless unit fixture proves only that a manually supplied row gets a note. Neither the native dispatch instructions nor the edit-pass handoff supplies the documented built-in case. [39-11-PLAN.md:136–140,197](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L136) · [39-04-PLAN.md:133](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L133) · [39-01-PLAN.md:164](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L164) · [39-15-PLAN.md:267](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L267) · [39-SPEC.md:303–315](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L303).
- **MEDIUM — The MCP picker loses an owner override when the server is absent from the config scan.** The picker deliberately allows a launch-form server found on the child PATH without host registration. But in that case, or when MCP config is `unknown`, the picker falls back directly to the seed role rather than the layered `[skills.roles]` value. Also, 39-12 starts its cleanup guarantee *after* directory creation, chmod, and file writing; failure during creation or writing can leave the run directory behind. [39-11-PLAN.md:167,171–172](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L167) · [39-01-PLAN.md:169–170,238–239](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L169) · [39-12-PLAN.md:198–202](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L198).
- **MEDIUM — The executor is left to repair lint and verification details.** Task 1’s prescribed `_read_body` has eight return paths against a cap of six; the plan calls for a split only in `check_lens`. Task 1’s named-skip failure is not observable through its command as written, and Task 3 duplicates the real `--describe` launch rather than reusing the group-supervised reader used by 39-06. The three tasks/four files meet the count limits, but Task 1 adds a runtime module, tracer and generated listings and reserves 10–13 minutes for its hook. Budget the combined implementation and hook time explicitly. [39-11-PLAN.md:98,139–147,201–218](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L98) · `pyproject.toml:75–80` · `tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:507–510,935–943` · `runner_facts.py:30–44`.

**Suggestions**
- Specify the exact checked-row contract in 39-11 and consume it in 39-12: scan name for display, runner name for flags and report matching, plus the validated path, form, and bytes. Add an integrated real-template tracer with a differing frontmatter name and a fixture for duplicate runner identities.
- Check the resolved `SKILL.md` before opening it and keep the body unchanged inside framing that cannot be mistaken for its contents. Make preview and launch use a stable validated input, then test both caps on the **final** composed prompt at equality and one byte over. Keep the synthetic 20,000-token case, but also use a legal-sized real lens that exceeds a small window.
- Resolve launch eligibility through the same layered role resolver even when the host has no registration. Put temp directory ownership around creation and writing as well as dispatch. Wire the built-in’s native-only handling explicitly, without enabling the edit pass by default.

**Risk assessment: HIGH.** The core helpers are reasonable, but the missing calls, file-boundary checks, and final byte accounting leave the promised behavior unproven on a real lane. Wave 5 has no peer; the seed, validator, parser, and renderer are all available through the declared dependency chain. The issue is the content of the handoff, not wave order.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** Needed for R5. Reuse the R8 validator and the runner’s established file-read and delivery contracts (`cross-ai.mjs:6422–6449,6520–6543`). Mirroring only the pieces needed for pre-checks is reasonable; allowing preview and launch to disagree on which bytes were checked is not a complete implementation. [39-11-PLAN.md:139–141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L139).
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Needed for the R1 amendment. The 0600 writer is sufficient; no general MCP launcher is needed. Reuse the table’s effective role resolution and make the 39-12 caller own cleanup from directory creation onward. [39-11-PLAN.md:171–172](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L171) · [39-01-PLAN.md:170](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L170).
- **LOW — Task 3:** The UI drift and closed-list checks are needed; reading the adapter constant by AST respects the frozen allowlist. Reuse `read_runner_describe` instead of adding another launch path, and keep behavioral fixtures for runner compatibility rather than relying on exact JS source text. [39-11-PLAN.md:200–205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L200) · `tests/test_arch.py:1632–1667` · `runner_facts.py:30–44`.

#### Batch disposition

**Revise before execution.** The release layout is sound: no same-wave edits collide in this batch; none of these plans adds a runner self-test case or touches the rendered copy; 39-17 owns the final re-render and timing refresh. Fix the wire schema and caller contracts first, then close the behavioral gaps before relying on the phase gates.

The SPEC rows not effectively covered by the traced handoffs are **R4 encoding** (all rendered paths validated), **R5 boundary and concurrency** (final argv fit and stable lens bytes), **R8 empty** (omitted file metadata is indistinguishable from an intentionally empty list because the dispatch flags default to `[]`), and **R11 adjacency** (the planned parity fixture has no lenses and does not exercise the broken handoffs or prompt-dependent budgeting). The corresponding **R3/R4 truthful-capability**, **R4 absent-binary**, **R5/R7/R8 whole-skip/no-silent-drop**, and **R8 embedded-value** prohibitions have named tests or owners, but those tests do not yet prove the stated behavior. The amended verified-path rule is correctly delegated to the runner; keep that boundary. [39-SPEC.md:548–575,586,604–609](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L548) · [39-12-PLAN.md:196–204,240](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L196) · [39-13-PLAN.md:171–173](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md#L171).

Reviewed all three manifest plans against checkout `9e3f11e` and their adjacent handoffs. No files were changed and no test suite, make target, or live host call was run; gate outcomes above are based on source inspection, not execution.
