#### Batch D (39-07, 39-10, 39-11)

#### 39-07

**Summary —** The Python-to-runner bridge is the right design for REV-04: it reuses the existing argv builder, preserves old callers when the new fields are empty, and reads capability facts from the runner rather than inferring host permissions in Python. The cycle-1 wire-shape fix is present. The remaining blocker is that the context-window handoff does not detect a context form already present in a real lane’s model ID.

**Strengths**
- Appending the new flags in `runner_flags` reaches both subprocess argv and the GSD hook string without adding a second builder. The independent golden protects the no-lens case, and the hook keeps its existing token allowlist. [39-07 plan:167–171](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L167) · [runner_dispatch.py:191–233](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L191)
- The pi and Copilot tracers exercise the actual Node preview and Python reader without starting either host. The preview already uses a process group and returns `None` on failures; the plan preserves `preview_setting`’s two-item result for the effort probe. [39-07 plan:124–135](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md#L124) · [runner_dispatch.py:296–333](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L296) · [effort_probe.py:58–68](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/effort_probe.py#L58)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — A context form already in the model ID can trigger a false refusal.** Task 1’s `context_form_applied` only looks at `setting context=` records. The normal Python lane constructor sets `context=None`; the runner returns immediately on a null context request, *before* checking whether the model ID already carries the bracket form. Meanwhile, the window lookup strips that suffix before reading the cached catalog. A Cursor lane configured with a bracketed context can therefore be capped at the bare model’s catalog value when the spec requires `unknown` unless the owner supplied an override. Task 2’s synthetic `id_carries` case does not reproduce this path. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md:132,165` · [runner_dispatch.py:99–106](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L99) · [cross-ai.mjs:2027–2043](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L2027) · [39-06 plan:140](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md#L140)
- **MEDIUM — The malformed-wire guard checks types, not the full contract.** `_label_ok` accepts any strings, including an invented state or blank source. The prompt check also permits `argv` with a null cap and `stdin` with a numeric cap, neither of which 39-02 emits. In particular, accepting `argv`/null silently removes the optional-lens argv budget in 39-12. The normalization test says it drops an extra *label* key but only plants an extra *top-level* key. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md:133,163–164` · [39-02 plan:146–153](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L146) · [39-12 plan:205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L205)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2’s offline rule is reactive.** Its bare `make` gates invoke `uv run`; “stop if it tries to download” does not prevent a network attempt. The same gap applies to the commit hooks. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-07-PLAN.md:91,177–189` · [Makefile:98–113](Makefile#L98) · [.pre-commit-config.yaml:20–43](.pre-commit-config.yaml#L20)

**Suggestions**
- Have the runner expose evidence for an already-carried context form without changing the existing setting lines. Add one real-Node fixture using an ordinary bracketed lane with no separate context request, plus the owner-override case at the window seam.
- Pin the label vocabulary and the allowed channel/cap pairs. Supply exact offline commands for both verification and commit, using the existing 39-02/39-12 wrapper pattern.

**Risk assessment — HIGH.** The implementation is small enough for the executor; the blocker is semantic. A normally configured lane can be refused before spawn on a window the project does not know.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** The bridge is required by R4. Keep `print_argv_lines` and `runner_flags`; put the missing context evidence next to the runner’s existing `bracketValue` logic, not in a second Python host parser. That keeps 39-06 and 39-12 on one source of truth. [runner_dispatch.py:215–233](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L215) · [cross-ai.mjs:1959–1967](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L1959)
- **LOW — Task 2:** The compatibility tests are needed. Extracting the existing golden once is sufficient; extend the malformed-record table rather than adding another parser test framework. [tests/test_runner_flags.py:33–53](tests/test_runner_flags.py#L33) · [tests/test_runner_preview.py:59–98](tests/test_runner_preview.py#L59)

#### 39-10

**Summary —** The fixed section order, no-shell golden, and shared formatter are good foundations for REV-04. The remaining problems are in what the formatter claims: relative PATH entries are discarded, an applied call-file can be mistaken for host-config access, and some rendered values bypass the safety rules.

**Strengths**
- The no-shell golden puts `rg` and the code-index binary on PATH anyway. That tests whether the block actually obeys `shell=no`, rather than passing because the binaries happen to be absent. [39-10 plan:122,170–172](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L122) · [detection.py:1351–1356](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/detection.py#L1351)
- `BlockInput` avoids a seven-argument formatter and an import cycle with execute dispatch. The summary returns text only, matching the current 39-13 caller, which adds the prefix. [39-10 plan:128,140–141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L128) · [39-13 plan:124–126](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md#L124) · [pyproject.toml:70–80](pyproject.toml#L70)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — The block still makes false confinement claims.** Task 1 labels `workspace` as “write inside the working tree only,” but the runner explicitly documents that Codex workspace-write can also allow `/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, and configured roots. For `unknown`, the plan correctly retains a soft instruction under D-04, but 39-12 feeds it a sentence that asserts there is **no** OS-level confinement. D-04’s trigger can stay unchanged without making that assertion. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:127,133` · [cross-ai.mjs:2173–2179](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L2173) · [execute_dispatch.py:39–49](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/execute_dispatch.py#L39) · [39-12 plan:239–242](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L239)
- **HIGH — Some prompt values bypass both redaction and the R8 check.** Task 1’s display rule calls `escape_value`, which escapes control characters but does not redact secrets. `_fact_line` bypasses even that helper for label sources. The soft-confinement sentence also embeds `target_dir` before validation: 39-12 checks file, skill and MCP paths, but not that sentence’s path. The named tooling-path omission fix is present; it does not cover these other inputs. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:130–139` · [39-03 plan:203–209](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md#L203) · [39-12 plan:151,239](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L151) · [secret_shapes.py:99–117](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/secret_shapes.py#L99)
- **HIGH — Host-config registration can win even when the final argv excludes it.** Task 1 allows the fallback whenever `mcp != none`. Claude’s applied MCP form includes `--strict-mcp-config`, which limits it to the call file. If that file contains another server, a host-config registration for the code index is not evidence that the index is available. Conversely, Copilot’s added file augments the user config, so rejecting every `call_file` fallback would also be wrong. Task 2 tests precedence when the selected index itself is applied, but not this distinction. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:136,168–169` · [cross-ai.mjs:1898,1928](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L1898)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2 locks in an incomplete child-PATH implementation.** Task 1 drops all relative entries; Task 2 explicitly expects `PATH=bin` not to find an executable in the child’s `bin`. That is not merely a missing fixture: 39-12 already has the child cwd but does not pass it to the block. Also, its unknown-runtime fixture deliberately requires code-index advice, whereas R4’s unknown-runtime acceptance says only unknown facts. The plan needs to distinguish a known child environment from an unverified one, rather than assume the caller’s environment describes a custom launcher. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:134,171,173` · [39-12 plan:141–152](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L141) · [39-SPEC.md:289–292](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L289)
- **MEDIUM — The executor has to finish contracts the tasks call exact.** The section helpers dereference the optional `BlockInput.facts` without a local guard or a narrowed argument; `build_block`’s guard does not narrow it inside those helpers. Task 2 says thirteen tests but specifies twelve, leaving interpretation to the lane. The final `make` gates also have the offline gap above. With a four-file tracer, a generated-file commit that runs full unit/arch, and over a dozen block fixtures overall, the 1,800-second fit should be measured, not assumed. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:89,128–140,168–195` · [pyproject.toml:104–108](pyproject.toml#L104) · [tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:227–230](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L227)

**Suggestions**
- Keep the formatter, but pass it validated inputs, the actual child cwd, and explicit evidence of whether the call’s MCP form replaces or augments host config. Reuse the same PATH resolver for 39-11.
- Use neutral confinement wording for unknown access; describe `workspace-write` without claiming an exact writable-root set. Pin those outputs, a redacted label source, a rejected target path, and the strict-versus-additive MCP cases.

**Risk assessment — HIGH.** Several incorrect claims would be systematic, not cosmetic. A lane would receive a block that looks authoritative while overstating confinement or advertising unavailable tools.

##### Ponytail findings
- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** The composer and summary are needed for R4/R11; the argument carrier earns its keep. The discarded `runtime` and `model` arguments in `summary_line` do not: 39-13 already owns the prefix. Reuse the existing code-index *wording*, but make its selection depend on the runner’s facts. [39-10 plan:136,141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L136) · [tooling_guidance.py:43–65](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/tooling_guidance.py#L43)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The matrix is justified, but many fixtures compare text against the same constants the implementation prints. Keep the independent pi golden and use targeted assertions for the branches that can make a false claim: relative PATH, unknown confinement, and strict MCP. Keep `gen_test_groups.py` as the only listing writer. [39-10 plan:168–183](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L168) · [tools/gen_test_groups.py:96–104](tools/gen_test_groups.py#L96)

#### 39-11

**Summary —** This plan owns the right pieces of R5 and R1: selecting lenses, budgeting whole files, and constructing the private MCP file from seed launch data. It is not yet ready for unattended execution. The prescribed reader fails one of its own fixtures, and the handoff can budget or report different lenses from the ones the runner delivers.

**Strengths**
- The real-Node tracer checks multibyte prose length and SHA-256, and distinguishes pi’s path form from Copilot’s prose form. It complements the existing runner harness, which already compares the full delivered prompt bytes. [39-11 plan:133](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L133) · [cross-ai-selftest.mjs:10233–10256](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-selftest.mjs#L10233)
- The MCP writer uses exclusive creation and mode `0600`, copies no host-config values, and emits no empty file that the runner would refuse. 39-12 explicitly takes ownership of cleanup from directory creation. [39-11 plan:171–172](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L171) · [cross-ai.mjs:6452–6465](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6452) · [39-12 plan:201](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L201)

**Concerns**
- **HIGH — Task 1’s file reader makes Task 3’s directory fixture fail.** `_read_raw` wraps the descriptor with `os.fdopen` *before* checking `fstat`. On this Linux host, wrapping a directory descriptor raises `IsADirectoryError` before the check executes; I verified that with a read-only stdlib probe. The prescribed code also only catches errors from `os.open`, not from the later stat/read. The runner checks the raw descriptor first and closes it in `finally`. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:139,197` · [cross-ai.mjs:6433–6449](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6433)
- **HIGH — Keeping `raw` does not make delivery use the checked bytes.** 39-11 saves a Python snapshot, but 39-12 passes the original paths, never compares the final preview hash and size to that snapshot, and computes the final budget from the old row. The runner opens the files again at launch. An intervening edit can change the delivered bytes, overflow the planned budget, or invalidate the lens. The acknowledged path check-then-use limit does not satisfy R5’s separate read-once backstop. The existing retry test edits a file *after* the runner took its snapshot; it does not cover the Python-to-runner handoff. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:140–141` · [39-12 plan:204–205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L204) · [39-SPEC.md:559,575](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L559) · [cross-ai-selftest.mjs:10261–10279](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-selftest.mjs#L10261)
- **HIGH — Distinct prechecked names can still produce the wrong runner reports.** The runner matches each `--skill-name` against the *first* directory or frontmatter match, and deduplicates real directories before matching. For example, if the first directory is `lens-a` with frontmatter `lens-b`, and a later directory has frontmatter `lens-a`, both pass the Python uniqueness test. Both name flags then bind to the first runner row, leaving no `lens-b` report. The two-lens tracer does not cross those aliases. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:133,140–141` · [cross-ai.mjs:6485–6511](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6485)
- **HIGH — A damaged role table can block a lane despite the scan fallback.** `lens_set` and `mcp_pick` independently reload the strict seed. A broken or missing seed tolerated by 39-04’s scan therefore raises again in 39-11. And 39-12 directly evaluates the strict `user_roles` reader before calling `mcp_pick`, undoing the malformed-config fallback as well. Even a list containing a non-object can pass `load_seed` and fail at the consumers’ `.get` calls. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:138,171` · [39-01 plan:169–171,203](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md#L169) · [39-04 plan:141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L141) · [39-12 plan:201](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L201)
- **HIGH — The `simplify` fix is reachable only in a helper test.** Task 3 explicitly checks that the default `review_lens` call leaves it out. The native composer and native prompt both use review-lens selection; the edit pass calls `lens_set` with `edit_lens` but without a native host, and runs only a subprocess. Thus no actual caller delivers fileless `simplify` to native Claude. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:138,199` · [39-12 plan:203–205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L203) · [39-14 plan:123–124](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L123) · [39-15 plan:166](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L166)
- **MEDIUM — MCP lookup and block lookup disagree on relative PATH.** Task 2 resolves relative entries in Python’s cwd, then makes the result absolute. The host runs in the requested cwd, while 39-10 drops those entries entirely. The result can be a different command in the MCP file from the one the lane would resolve, not just a missing display line. Task 2 tests different parent/child PATH strings, but not different working directories. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:167,171` · [39-10 plan:134](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L134) · [runner_dispatch.py:651–656](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py#L651)
- **MEDIUM — The exact budget and cap checks stop short of what is sent.** Task 3’s equality tests use synthetic size rows. The final composer still charges rows by the Python estimate, even when the preview reports a lens ignored. It can also drop the last delivered lens without retrying a smaller one rejected in the draft pass. A skipped lens’s note can push an otherwise fitting base over the window. Pin the final payload, not just the helper arithmetic. Task 1 is also the heaviest executor task here: reader, name mapping, budget, real-Node tracer and full-hook commit; Task 3 repeats the bare offline-sensitive gates. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:99,136–154,198,207–219` · [39-12 plan:204–205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L204)

**Suggestions**
- Fix descriptor handling in the tracer commit and put the non-regular-file and read-error cases there, not two tasks later.
- Pass one validated scan result into both selection paths. Define one identity contract for directory aliases and runner names, and one delivery contract for checked, previewed and launched bytes. Test each at the real runner seam without a real host call.
- Add a caller-level native Claude test and a non-native named-note case. Keep editing lenses out of ordinary review instructions; make the built-in behavior explicit at the appropriate native entry point.
- Add a final-payload boundary fixture and an offline, timed commit path. If the tracer cannot fit the cap with hooks enabled, split it into buildable commits or route it natively.

**Risk assessment — HIGH.** There is a reproducible verification failure plus gaps that can change what a lane receives. A green happy-path preview would not establish the locked delivery, budget, or fallback behavior.

##### Ponytail findings
- **HIGH — Task 1:** The precheck and budget are required, but carrying `raw` that no downstream caller consumes adds memory without preserving delivery. Reuse the runner’s descriptor-check order and its existing prompt-byte harness; either use the snapshot through delivery or do not claim it protects delivery. [cross-ai.mjs:6433–6449](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6433) · [cross-ai-selftest.mjs:10245–10256](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-selftest.mjs#L10245)
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The exclusive writer is needed. Reloading the seed and resolving PATH differently from the block are not. Reuse the guarded scan inputs and one child-cwd-aware lookup; leave cleanup with 39-12. [39-04 plan:141](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L141) · [39-12 plan:201](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L201)
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** The drift and disjointness tests are justified, and `read_runner_describe` is the right existing helper. The extension drift pin only compares constants: the adapter lowercases its input, while the proposed selector does not. Add an uppercase-extension fixture and spend the rest of the test budget on the actual delivery handoff. ##### Status: Issues Found — fix and re-invoke

Reviewed all three manifest plans against checkout `9e3f11e`. No files were changed, no test suites or make targets were run, and live-host behavior and executor timing remain unverified.

#### Batch F (39-15, 39-16, 39-18, 39-19)

#### 39-15

**Summary.** The subprocess cutover and the ordered documentation tables are useful. The main gap is that the shipped skill would still run a different lens-selection flow for native reviewers, and its path and rollback promises are broader than the implementation it documents.

**Strengths**

- The plan updates both the initial dispatch and the resume argv rather than just changing the first call. It preserves the existing JSON argv replay, which avoids reinterpreting saved paths as shell syntax. See [39-15-PLAN.md:123–136](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L123) and [cross-ai-dispatch.md:286–303](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-dispatch.md#L286).
- The role test compares code, seed data, and ordered Markdown rows in one place. This follows the existing documentation-test pattern and does not require a new generated test-group entry. See [39-15-PLAN.md:272–280](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L272) and [tests/test_cross_ai_docs_text.py:140–157](tests/test_cross_ai_docs_text.py#L140).

**Concerns**

1. **HIGH — Task 1 bypasses the native lens builder.** The proposed `skill-table` recipe has no `--cwd`, host-resolution rule, or missing-helper branch. It also rebuilds the UI extension list and selects only disk-backed review/UI rows, so it cannot handle the locked file-less built-in outcome. In a different worktree it can read the wrong project’s roles; on a partial install it calls a helper that the native fallback explicitly allows to be absent. The earlier plan already exposes `capability-block --native`, although its default review-role selection must also be checked against the `edit_lens`-seeded `simplify` case. See [39-15-PLAN.md:166](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L166), [cross-ai-dispatch.md:52–58](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai-dispatch.md#L52), [39-04-PLAN.md:142–143](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md#L142), [39-11-PLAN.md:199](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L199), and [39-12-PLAN.md:153–154,203–205](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L203).
2. **HIGH — Passing `--files` does not cover all the paths the template embeds.** The subprocess template inserts the checklist, framework profile, and grounding roots independently of `DOC_PATHS` and `GROUNDING_DOCS`; the planned validator checks the supplied file list, lens paths, and MCP path, not those other substitutions. Conversely, passing *all* documents to a lane whose `--cwd` is the first document’s worktree will refuse a supported labeled multi-worktree input. Neither case fails the proposed substring pin. See [39-15-PLAN.md:123–135,171](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L123), [reviewer-prompts.md:17–32](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md#L17), [ai-kit-spec-review/SKILL.md:321–322](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/SKILL.md#L321), and [39-12-PLAN.md:151,207](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L151).
3. **HIGH — Task 2 overpromises what rejection restores.** “A rejection restores the recorded bytes of the listed files” omits that an owner-modified file is deliberately left alone, a partial reject returns nonzero and retains the record, and edits outside the approved list are not recorded. A lane asked to edit only `a.txt` can still touch `b.txt`; rejecting is not a whole-tree rollback. See [39-15-PLAN.md:260–265](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L260) and [39-14-PLAN.md:118,127,163–165](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L118).
4. **MEDIUM — Task 1 turns conditional tool guidance into a claim.** The proposed text treats `shell: unknown` as sufficient for “usable,” and tells the lane to use the graph when the section names it. The builder explicitly makes shell use conditional and says an applied MCP argument does **not** prove the server started. The wire description also says *every* `--argv-file` field is unknown, while the producer still supplies a `prompt` object. See [39-15-PLAN.md:145–167](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L145), [39-10-PLAN.md:127,135](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md#L127), and [39-02-PLAN.md:152,185](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md#L152).
5. **MEDIUM — Executor isolation and eval setup are not fully specified.** The per-module HOME protects the Python test children, not the parent `make` or its `uv run` hooks; the bare commands do not enforce offline mode or the repository TMPDIR. Also, both new review evals name `demo.md` with no fixture or setup, even though the skill stops on a missing input. The shape test accepts `files: []`; that does not exercise either branch. See [39-15-PLAN.md:83–85,268–292](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L268), [Makefile:98–113](Makefile#L98), [tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:584–589](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L584), [tests/test_skill_evals_shape.py:57–65](tests/test_skill_evals_shape.py#L57), and the existing [fixture-backed eval:5–8](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/evals/evals.json#L5).

**Suggestions**

- Give the executor one authoritative native command, with explicit project root, host resolution, and a no-helper fallback. Pin the real selection result, not just the presence of `LENSES`.
- Settle the path policy with 39-12 before freezing both fresh and resume snippets. Specify one argv element per path, how `none` is omitted, and which paths are legal in a labeled multi-root review. Add a fixture that asserts both validated argv and no spawn on rejection.
- Document the actual reject limits. Reuse the active-project eval fixture, make the simulated approval/refusal explicit, and reuse the offline/TMPDIR wrapper pattern in [39-12-PLAN.md:139](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L139).

**Risk Assessment — HIGH.** This is mostly documentation, but the documentation is the caller. Incorrect instructions can bypass the intended validation, stop a previously supported review, or give the owner a false rollback guarantee. Each task is at or below five files; no source-grounded evidence establishes a 1,800-second overrun.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** The cutover is required by R4/R5/R8/R12. Delete the second native selection recipe rather than maintaining its own extension list and host ordering beside [39-11-PLAN.md:137–140](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L137). Keep the existing resume JSON path.
- **LOW — Task 2:** Keep the required ordered tables and reuse the parsers/tests already in [tests/test_cross_ai_docs_text.py:27–37](tests/test_cross_ai_docs_text.py#L27). The extra `Counter` assertion repeats within-table uniqueness already proved by ordered equality; its only additional check is cross-list duplication, which belongs with the existing closed-list disjointness check.

#### 39-16

**Summary.** The sorter opt-in and target-selection fixes are present, and the dated notes have valid anchors. The capability line, however, is described rather than wired into the actual report step, and the role-write flow has no defined failure path.

**Strengths**

- Task 1 now waits for the announced destination and uses the exact project/global flags exposed by 39-08. The existing replacement gate stops without writing on “stop.” See [39-16-PLAN.md:133–142](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L133), [39-08-PLAN.md:177–178](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L177), and [spec-config/SKILL.md:161–169](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/SKILL.md#L161).
- Task 2’s four anchors match the current requirement and roadmap text. The later gate checks the same prefixes without adding `.planning/` reads to the test suite. See [39-16-PLAN.md:185–204](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L185), [.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md:280–304](.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md#L280), and [.planning/ROADMAP.md:973–977](.planning/ROADMAP.md#L973).

**Concerns**

1. **HIGH — Step 4 never runs the command Task 1 says it runs.** Task 1 adds one sentence to Step 2.5, not an action to Step 4. The existing report reads the saved roster and handles trust; it has no `config-status` call. That command requires `--cwd`, and 39-13 emits capability lines only for `active`. A saved command-bearing roster can remain `pending` after the owner declines trust, so “every saved lane” is also too broad. The test only checks that two strings occur somewhere. See [39-16-PLAN.md:145,152](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L145), [spec-config/SKILL.md:376–445](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/SKILL.md#L376), [config_status.py:89–99](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py#L89), and [39-13-PLAN.md:118,126](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md#L118).
2. **MEDIUM — The optional sorter has no failure or partial-write instructions.** A malformed roles table stops classification with exit 2. A confirm can fail on confinement or IO; if the second of three confirmations fails, the first may already be saved. The proposed flow neither distinguishes those outcomes from “jev absent” nor says what Step 4 reports. Also, the short capability sentence should not imply that the whole `check-reviewer` invocation is a dry run: only its capability field is; the existing command calls the usability probe. See [39-16-PLAN.md:125–145](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L125), [39-08-PLAN.md:139–140,176–178](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-08-PLAN.md#L176), and [cli.py:1334–1346](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py#L1334).
3. **MEDIUM — The wizard’s linked config reference would disagree with its main flow.** `references/config-file.md` is specifically loaded for questions about other tables and says the wizard writes only `[lanes]`. Task 1 updates the design document but leaves that reference untouched. See [39-16-PLAN.md:103–112,146–149](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L103) and [spec-config/references/config-file.md:3,16–32](skills/ai-kit-spec-config/references/config-file.md#L16).
4. **MEDIUM — The headless execution contract is ambiguous.** Task 1 says to substitute whatever flags `--help` prints, while the objective requires exact text and stopping on a mismatch; the tests still pin the original spelling. Task 2’s bare `make` commands also have no offline wrapper, despite the scratch-HOME rule. See [39-16-PLAN.md:81–86,105–111,205–213](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md#L105) and [Makefile:98–113](Makefile#L98).

**Suggestions**

- Put the exact `config-status --cwd <project root>` invocation at the report/trust boundary and specify what to show for `pending`, `none`, and nonzero exit. Pin it inside Step 4, not across the whole file.
- State what happens when classification fails, when a skill is `not offered`, and when only some confirms succeed. Use the existing report’s confirmed/not-confirmed distinction.
- Bring the linked config reference into scope; if that takes Task 1 over five files, move a small docs edit to Task 2 rather than asking the executor to discover scope. Treat mismatched CLI help as a contract failure and reuse the offline wrapper.

**Risk Assessment — MEDIUM.** The atomic writer is not being changed here, but the user can miss execution-lane capability evidence or mistake a partially completed role write for a completed setup.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** R2/R11/R12 need this change. Reuse the existing Step 4 report and [tests/test_spec_config_flow_text.py:246–250](tests/test_spec_config_flow_text.py#L246) to pin the actual step; no second roster reader or preview formatter is needed.
- **LOW — Task 2:** D-19 requires the notes. Keep the four fixed-string checks; no parser, new module, or generated files are needed. The existing Make targets are the right gates once their execution environment is explicit.

#### 39-18

**Summary.** The judge loop correctly reuses the existing verdict machinery and distinguishes a judged result from a no-rejudge fix. Several orchestration failures can still stop a valid run or misclassify a result, including one guaranteed failure on the fix path.

**Strengths**

- Using `bar_with_base`, not the headline percent, is correct. The prior review report demonstrates why: its headline is 92.8, but its other valid verdict is 83.2; the implementation compares lowest valid scores on the matching rubric. See [39-18-PLAN.md:147](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L147), [.planning/judge/REJUDGE.md:56–57](.planning/judge/REJUDGE.md#L56), and [tools/judge_loop.py:1619–1636](tools/judge_loop.py#L1619).
- The scoped gate now includes historical judge IDs. That is necessary because the checker validates records across rounds and rejects a judge absent from the allowed list. See [39-18-PLAN.md:149](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L149) and [tools/judge_loop.py:1298–1331](tools/judge_loop.py#L1298).

**Concerns**

1. **HIGH — Every prescribed fix run includes a nonexistent test.** `tests.test_lane_capability_docs` is not in the checkout and no earlier plan creates it. Cycle 1 moved the pins to `tests.test_cross_ai_docs_text`, which the same command already includes. The runner treats each explicit argument as a module and propagates failures. See [39-18-PLAN.md:83,150](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L150), [39-15-PLAN.md:43–44](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md#L43), and [tools/run_unittest_parallel.py:889–911](tools/run_unittest_parallel.py#L889).
2. **HIGH — Real-HOME authorization and migration checks are narrower than the launch.** The cited `AGENTS.md` rule requires a judge result; it does not explicitly authorize live probes with the caller’s HOME. The copied preflight also uses the passwd HOME, does not trim XDG, and checks only root `.planning/config.json`; the runner uses the child’s `HOME`, trims XDG, and can migrate scoped GSD config. Thus the preflight can print `legacy retry: none` for a launch that would migrate a file. See [39-18-PLAN.md:107,118–121](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L107), [AGENTS.md:65–79](AGENTS.md#L65), [legacy_retry_check.py:24–33](tmp/judge-38.1/legacy_retry_check.py#L24), and [cross-ai.mjs:2737–2745,3078–3082,3631–3642](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L3631).
3. **HIGH — Subtracting refutation rows does not count remaining serious findings.** A duplicate refutation, or a valid refutation of a medium finding, reduces the proposed count even though neither removes another critical/important/high finding. Four serious findings can incorrectly enter the no-rejudge `fix` branch. The checker already matches skill, round, judge and finding ID and deduplicates IDs. See [39-18-PLAN.md:149](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L149), [findings.py:23–38](tmp/judge-38.1/findings.py#L23), and [tools/judge_loop.py:817–830](tools/judge_loop.py#L817).
4. **MEDIUM — A finished judge process is not necessarily a scored lane.** Task 1 now waits for substitute exit notifications, which fixes the earlier race. But `_cmd_ingest` returns 0 even for “attempt recorded without a verdict,” and `status` considers failed worker lanes ended. Task 2’s pool check prevents bad publication, but the plan still declares Task 1 done without saying how an unscored substitute terminates the attempt. Also, a worker that dies before recording its first attempt can leave a `pending` lane, so the repeated 170-second waits have no terminal branch. See [39-18-PLAN.md:119–129](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L119), [lanes.py:201–208,246–253,360–375,447–466](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L246), and [tools/judge_loop.py:665–674,1949–1954](tools/judge_loop.py#L1949).
5. **MEDIUM — The copied count command can invoke live jev as a fallback.** `lanes.py count` does not pass `--no-jev`; the judge tool discovers a client and can ask it if a deterministic trigger does not choose two. The currently planned skills have qualifying historical signals, but an optional skill or a changed base can enter the fallback. That conflicts with the judge-only live-call boundary. See [39-18-PLAN.md:119](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-18-PLAN.md#L119), [lanes.py:386–388](tmp/judge-38.1/lanes.py#L386), and [tools/judge_loop.py:1811–1825,1908–1925](tools/judge_loop.py#L1908).

**Suggestions**

- Remove the obsolete module from both references and retain the one existing doc-contract suite. For the finding count, filter the actual findings with `refuted_ids` instead of subtracting input rows.
- Require a source that explicitly authorizes the live judge calls and their real-HOME scope, or record JDG-04 blocked. Match migration preflight inputs to the launch environment, including scoped paths; check the audit’s exit before publishing.
- Give Task 1 a bounded stalled-worker branch and require a scored substitute record, not just a zero exit. Make count’s no-jev mode explicit and keep `pool_check.py before` as the stop if coverage is below two.

**Risk Assessment — HIGH.** There is a predictable fix-path failure, a decision-count error at the three-finding boundary, and a host-config authorization gap. These can be fixed without changing the judge bar, judge pair, or three-round policy.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** The frozen-tree and two-judge checks are needed. Reuse the existing lane and pool drivers, but use [pool_check.py:43–65](tmp/judge-38.1/pool_check.py#L43) as the scored-coverage check at handoff rather than extending the meaning of `status`.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** The records, scoped gate, and fix commits are required. Reuse [tools/judge_loop.py:817–830](tools/judge_loop.py#L817) for refutations and the existing doc suite for fixes. Do not add a second bar calculator or a replacement test module.

#### 39-19

**Summary.** The plan has the right closing checks: fixed amendment markers, resource preflight, one guarded full suite, and a stat-only audit. The new candidate file is not yet the source of truth for the gate, and the timeout path can run cleanup before the process it is meant to clean up has ended.

**Strengths**

- The five Python-group lines plus the installer line match the actual Make targets and the checker’s non-vacuous result requirements. See [39-19-PLAN.md:126–128](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L126), [Makefile:59–89](Makefile#L59), and [check_gate.py:25–45](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/check_gate.py#L25).
- The informational phase-start comparison and the blocking within-gate audit are separate. The latter compares nanosecond mtimes and detects a listed path appearing or disappearing without opening a config file. See [39-19-PLAN.md:101,129,137–138](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L101) and [real_config_audit.py:16–42](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/real_config_audit.py#L16).

**Concerns**

1. **HIGH — The gate records a candidate but its helpers use moving HEAD.** `run_gate.py`, `wait_gate.py`, and `check_gate.py` each independently resolve HEAD. If HEAD moves while the run is active, the waiter can look for the wrong result; if HEAD moves and returns before the final check, the final check does not show the tests ran on the pinned tree throughout. The presence check for `guard-<sha>.out` also prevents only a second *completed* run, not two starts. See [39-19-PLAN.md:99,127–130](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L99), [run_gate.py:11–21](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/run_gate.py#L11), [wait_gate.py:8–16](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/wait_gate.py#L8), and [check_gate.py:67–75](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/check_gate.py#L67).
2. **HIGH — A bounded wait is not bounded teardown.** On timeout the plan stops the outer task and immediately runs the survivor check. The guard launches `make` in a separate session and shows no signal-handler cleanup path for cancellation. A host that stops the outer task without stopping that session can leave work running while the plan considers removing TMPDIR. The final survivor wording also still promises “no surviving process,” although the detector only matches selected argv and cwd patterns. See [39-19-PLAN.md:127–129,147,174](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L127), [orch-guard.py:35–66](tmp/run-2026-11-19/orch-guard.py#L35), and [survivors.py:12–21,35–59](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/survivors.py#L35).
3. **MEDIUM — The final audit and offline scope do not cover the whole declared gate.** The audit compare runs before the final `check-dogfood`, which reads the real GSD consent state. The copied suite wrapper also does not set `UV_OFFLINE`, while several `make test` prerequisites invoke `uv run`. An unprovisioned cache can trigger network access instead of a preflight stop. See [39-19-PLAN.md:68,129–130](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L129), [make_test.sh:1–5](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/make_test.sh#L1), [Makefile:64–85](Makefile#L64), and [tools/check_dogfood.py:334–346](tools/check_dogfood.py#L334).
4. **MEDIUM — The closing handoff has no path for the owner’s install rule.** The plan hands off the four SUMMARY sections to verification but has no evidence slot for a scratch-HOME installer/hook check, or for the required live install/recheck being blocked by consent. The installer suite is not that live recheck. See [39-19-PLAN.md:173–179](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-19-PLAN.md#L173) and [AGENTS.md:347–351](AGENTS.md#L347). Keep live work out of this no-consent run; don’t let the gate imply it happened.

**Suggestions**

- Have the three helpers use `candidate.txt`; check SHA and tracked cleanliness at suite start, suite exit, and after dogfood. Use a single run claim and define what an interrupted or failed run leaves behind.
- Require confirmed teardown of the owned test run before deleting scratch. State `no matching survivors detected` in the done and success fields as well as the SUMMARY.
- Make the provisioned/offline preflight explicit. Put the blocking audit after the last real-environment command, or label it strictly as the `make test` interval. Hand the verifier an explicit blocked or pending install/recheck item.

**Risk Assessment — HIGH.** A clean sequential run has a plausible happy path, but the plan can misattribute evidence or leave processes behind on interruption—the exact cases a final gate should handle.

##### Ponytail findings

- **LOW — Task 1:** The dated-note, added-line, and stat checks are required. Reuse [added_line_scan.py:44–55](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/added_line_scan.py#L44) and [phase_mtimes.py:17–44](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-14/phase_mtimes.py#L17). Do not add a second diff parser or broaden the scan’s evidence claim.
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** Keep one `make test`, its guard, and its existing log checker. Use one candidate value and one teardown path rather than adding another watcher or another full suite.

##### Cross-plan coverage and handoffs

- **No same-wave collision found.** Waves 9, 10, 12, and 13 each contain one plan. The shared doc test is serialized through 39-15 → 39-16; the sorter is now an explicit 39-16 dependency; 39-17’s render precedes 39-18; and 39-19 explicitly requires 39-18’s summary. The stale doc-test name in 39-18 is a handoff error, not a missing dependency.
- **SPEC coverage still unproven at four seams.** R5’s read-once backstop is not met by one Python read: the runner rereads the source at preview/launch ([SPEC:559](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L559), [39-11:31](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md#L31), [cross-ai.mjs:6485–6503](skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs#L6485)). R8’s missing-list edge has a helper test, but `--files` defaults to `[]` at the dispatch boundary and other template paths are not forwarded ([SPEC:572,609](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L572), [39-12:151,207](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md#L151)). R9’s no-unaccepted-edits prohibition is not enforced for unlisted files ([SPEC:610](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md#L610), [39-14:118,127](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md#L118)). The R3 template-versus-render capability-line comparison was handed to 39-17, but `render.py check` checks pristine bytes and the prior dry render, not an invocation of both copies with the same fixture argv ([39-17:171–176](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-17-PLAN.md#L171), [render.py:153–161](tmp/run-2026-11-19/p381-12/render.py#L153)). Assign or explicitly resolve these before treating a green closing suite as complete coverage.
- **Known deferred R10 boundary:** 39-09 covers a missing `skill_table` module, not an entirely absent review package. `build_inventory` returns before reaching the new optional field when the sibling `gsd_tools` module is unavailable. Keep that distinction in verification rather than claiming the full missing-package acceptance passed ([39-09:28](.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-09-PLAN.md#L28), [host.py:122–128](skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/host.py#L122), [inventory.py:469–476](skills/ai-kit-gsd-curated-config/ai_kit_gsd_curated_config/inventory.py#L469)).

**Verdict: fix the HIGH findings before execution.** This was a static, read-only review. No tests, make targets, live lanes, or host-config probes were run, and no files were changed.

#### Batch E (39-12, 39-13, 39-14)

#### 39-12

##### Summary

The shared composer is the right design for REV-04. The current plan does wire review, execute, and the standalone command to it, and it preserves the distinction between a failed preview and an actual refusal. The remaining blockers are at the boundaries: callers can omit values that need validation, and the window and lens checks are not consistently based on the final dispatch.

##### Strengths

- Reuses `print_argv_lines`, which is already bounded, read-only, and runs Node in its own process group. It does not add a second Python parser for host flags. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:296-318`
- Moves composition after the native and skip-route checks, and puts the new refusal catch ahead of the generic `ValueError` catch. That preserves the existing no-report failure convention without mislabeling exit 65 as a runner exit. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:145,206-207`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1310-1322`
- Owns the MCP directory from creation and tests cleanup on a publication failure. The extra allowed root is limited to the generated MCP value, not added to the file-list policy. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:151,190-191,201`

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — R8 still does not cover the actual embedded values.** `--files` defaults to `[]`; `_value_refusal` enumerates only the supplied list and selected flags. A missing list therefore passes silently, contrary to the locked missing-list edge. The blank-prompt and native branches also bypass validation. The reviewer template embeds checklist and framework-profile paths, but 39-15 only forwards document and grounding paths; the Superpowers adapter forwards no file list at all. Finally, execute interpolates `target_dir` into the confinement sentence without validating it. An in-root safe fixture and one newline in `--files` do not prove those callers are protected. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:388-400,572`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:151-154,205-207,239-241`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md:123,135`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/reviewer-prompts.md:17-30`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/dispatch_injection.py:383-396`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/execute_dispatch.py:39-49`
- **HIGH — An actual context-bearing argv can still use the wrong window.** `spec_with_effort` sets `context=None`. The runner emits no context setting line when that label is absent, even when the cursor model id already includes a context bracket. The catalog lookup strips the bracket, so the planned boolean can be false and the ordinary catalog window can refuse a lane whose effective window should be unknown. The new test inserts a report line the real runner does not emit in this case. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:195,204-205`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-06-PLAN.md:140`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:91-106`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1764-1783,2027-2043`
- **HIGH — The final lens budget is not the size of what is sent.** `_final` counts the same precomputed prose-block size for context and argv, even for an ignored or path-loaded lens; it reads neither the final prose byte counts nor the checksums. That can skip an admissible lens or refuse a lane based on bytes it will not receive. The draft also omits the SKILLS section and the retry only drops the tail, so it cannot reconsider a later, smaller lens against the final block. The current tests set the caps low enough to reject the lens in the *draft*, and do not exercise the claimed final-fit correction. The runner rereads the file at actual dispatch, so R5’s read-once backstop is still not met by a Python-only snapshot. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:186-188,204-205`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:140-141`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:325-329,555-559`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6495-6503,6525-6543,7083-7085`
- **HIGH — Dispatch bypasses the scan’s agreed fallback.** 39-04 catches invalid `[skills.roles]` and broken seed data. The composer then calls strict `user_roles` again for MCP, and `lens_set` and `mcp_pick` load the seed again. A syntactically valid config with an invalid role can therefore stop an otherwise runnable lane instead of falling back with a note. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-04-PLAN.md:141`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-01-PLAN.md:170,242`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:138,171`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:201-205`
- **MEDIUM — PATH lookup can resolve a different executable than the lane would.** The block discards relative PATH entries, while the MCP picker resolves them against Python’s cwd. The runner resolves them against the lane cwd, including empty segments. A project-relative `bin` entry with different caller and lane directories therefore makes the block incomplete or the MCP file select the wrong binary. This is a concrete D-05 gap, not a request to probe more hosts. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:134-136`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-11-PLAN.md:171`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:150,201`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:2695-2704,5351-5356,5692`
- **MEDIUM — Tasks 2 and 3 are not mechanically ready for the executor.** They touch eight and seven files respectively. Task 2’s prescribed composer exceeds the 15-local cap and leaves its split to the executor; Task 3 still constructs `_runner_spec` twice despite the triage disposition. Task 2 also tries to remove a docstring sentence that spans two lines using a one-line anchor, and the frontmatter still says `LaneRefused` is *not* a `ValueError`. The make wrapper sets the tripwire and offline flags but does not set scratch `HOME`; the commit wrapper is not specified. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:43,139,145,175,205-208,225,239-242`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/reviewer_dispatch.py:3-6`; `pyproject.toml:75-80`; `Makefile:98-113`

##### Suggestions

- Make the caller contract explicit: distinguish a missing list from an explicitly empty one, carry every structured path the caller interpolates, and validate it before any runner consumes it. Add real-composer refusal tests for the adapter, the native preview, and a valid in-root symlink whose target path is rejected. Reuse the scan’s tolerant result rather than reopening seed and role configuration.
- Use final runner evidence for context-form detection and prose size; count path-loaded content against context, not argv. Add fixtures for a context bracket with no explicit context flag, a lens that fits the draft but not the final block, and exact argv-cap equality. Assign snapshot consistency through preview and dispatch explicitly; don’t describe the current reread as read-once.
- Give the executor an exact split, test edits, and scratch/offline commit command. Move any over-cap task to the orchestrator if it cannot be made small enough within the locked waves.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** The new refusal path is sound in isolation, but the proposed integration can miss invalid values, reject a valid lane, or fail ordinary dispatch on an optional configuration error.

##### Ponytail findings

- **LOW — Task 1:** The tracer is needed for R4/R11. Reuse the existing parser/command registry (`skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py:89-102`) and generator (`tools/gen_test_groups.py:96-104`); no extra registration layer is needed.
- **HIGH — Task 2:** Review integration is needed, but a second strict config read and a draft/final size calculation with different assumptions add code without making it safer. Use the existing scan fallback and the runner’s `skill-block` receipt; carry the resulting contract into both the review call and the 39-15 instructions. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:201-208`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:6542`
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** Execute integration is needed. Build the existing `_runner_spec` once and pass it through. Keep confinement coverage, but make the real adapter and its files part of the test rather than stubbing composition at every caller. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:239-242`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/execute_dispatch.py:100-103`

#### 39-13

##### Summary

The plan preserves the `config-status` header and the single `check-reviewer` JSON object, which is a good compatibility choice. The unresolved issues are substantive: the summary can misstate a stored lane’s route, the execute parity test cannot pass as specified, and the adapter’s refusal can be classified as success.

##### Strengths

- Reuses `cfg_resolve(..., migrate=False)` for the roster, preserving the existing trust and migration rules. Builds summaries before writing stdout, so a failure cannot leave half of the new output behind. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:125-127`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:582-589,626-639`
- Keeps the refusal catch ahead of the report fallback and uses a cross-skill import already allowed by the architecture test. Both are worth preserving when the caller contract is fixed. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:180-184`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/cli.py:345-354`; `tests/test_arch.py:1655-1663`

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — A refused adapter task is classified as `ok`.** Task 3 puts the bare refusal record on stdout and returns 65. The shipped calling instructions use the exit file as a completion marker, then classify any nonempty JSON. The classifier finds a string instead of a runner-status object, defaults a missing `returncode` to `0`, and returns `ok`. That branch updates the escalation anchor and clears resume state even though no implementation ran. The new test stops at the shim and cannot catch this. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:180,183`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/references/dispatch-external-cli.md:84-99,109-128`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/dispatch_injection.py:442-469`
- **HIGH — The promised execute byte-equality test contradicts the implementation.** `capability-block` passes no soft-confinement sentence; `dispatch-execute` supplies it. The formatter inserts it for `write=full` or `unknown`, covering actual execute lanes. Using pi **review** capability lines for both calls can hide the mismatch. The standalone command also cannot receive the dispatch’s knobs or base prompt, so identical runtime/model/role/files are not sufficient at a lens-budget boundary. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:178-179`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:153-154,239`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-10-PLAN.md:133`
- **HIGH — The saved-native test cannot work, and malformed saved lanes can break status.** `cfg_resolve` removes stored `route` fields by design. Actual native routing also checks whether the current host can select the model; `lane_summary` never does. Separately, the config reader intentionally leaves a non-string runtime untouched. A nonempty list can therefore reach `runtime_id`’s dict lookup and raise instead of producing a status line. A non-mapping `extra` can similarly fail at `**extra`. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:116-126`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_io.py:151-167,181-199,680-691`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/routing.py:138-151,168-179`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/detection.py:175-177`
- **MEDIUM — Tasks 1–3 leave deterministic verification and isolation gaps.** Task 2’s `rg` expects a direct `lane_summary` assignment that the action does not write. Adding its four locals to the current handler also exceeds the 15-local cap as written. The herdr-identity test exercises active `config-status` in **Task 1**, and only fakes the old effort preview; waiting for a failure in Task 2 is too late to prevent a real Node dry run. The optional test edit is not declared in the file list. Task 3 still invokes bare `make`, whose lint recipes call `uv run`, despite the claimed offline fix. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:124-131,143-162,186-198`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cli.py:1324-1352`; `tests/test_config_herdr_identity.py:93-111`; `pyproject.toml:75-80`; `Makefile:98-113`
- **MEDIUM — The printed summary escapes controls but does not redact secrets or say it is a base-argv preview.** That matters because the new line prints raw config fields and deliberately omits call-scoped lens and MCP flags, while the 39-16 handoff describes the result as what the final argv lets the lane do. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:119,125,154`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-03-PLAN.md:203,208`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-16-PLAN.md:145`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/secret_shapes.py:99-113`

##### Suggestions

- Test refusal from the shim **through the shipped classifier and caller branch**. Preserve exit 65, the named reason, and the no-report outcome; pin that it never advances the task or clears resume state. Update the adapter reference in the same task.
- Give the parity command and dispatch the same inputs for the same lane. At minimum, use an execute fixture with `write=unknown` or `full`, a non-default knob, and one lens whose delivery changes at the final budget boundary.
- Reuse the existing pure route rules with read-only inputs, not the refreshing route walk. Label unverified or invalid entries honestly, redact displayed config values, and fix the exact test, lint, and staging instructions before dispatching the task.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** The adapter can advance a failed implementation as successful, and the current tests either mask or contradict the behavior they are meant to prove.

##### Ponytail findings

- **MEDIUM — Task 1:** Per-lane summaries are required. Reuse the pure route checks in `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/routing.py:138-179` instead of inventing a stored-route rule. Six files including both generated listings exceed the usual five-file task budget; keep generation, but make the fixture and all affected callers explicit up front. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:106-131`
- **MEDIUM — Task 2:** One JSON key is appropriately small. Reuse `review_spec` for review knobs and keep the capability dry run separate from the existing quota/effort probe, which really can spawn a lane. Stub both in affected tests. `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:109-114`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/quota.py:251-287`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:149-162`
- **HIGH — Task 3:** The adapter catch and parity checks are needed. Reuse the existing failure-classification boundary rather than introducing a payload it interprets as success. The existing adapter reference is a required caller, not optional documentation. `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/ai_kit_spec_superpowers/dispatch_injection.py:437-469`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-execute-superpowers/references/dispatch-external-cli.md:84-117`

#### 39-14

##### Summary

The owner-started, default-off command is the right Phase 39 deliverable; Phase 40 still owns its placement in the review loop. The plan correctly distinguishes a runner lock from other exit-75 outcomes. It is not yet safe to run on an owner’s working tree: the scope is advisory, and the recovery record does not protect the whole run–accept–reject lifecycle.

##### Strengths

- Uses a literal boolean opt-in, checks the final preview for `workspace` or `full`, and branches on `status == "locked"` rather than the shared exit code. The runner acquires that lock before execution, so discarding a record on the named pre-spawn lock result is justified. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:119,126,157-160`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:7162-7169`
- Keeps pre-pass content as bytes and tests non-UTF-8 rollback. Deferring CLI registration until both decision paths exist is a worthwhile guard on the intermediate commits. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:102,112,121,168,194-196`

##### Concerns

- **HIGH — Reject can leave unapproved lane changes in place.** `EDIT_PROMPT` asks the lane to edit only approved files, but `MSG_SCOPE` explicitly leaves other edits to the owner. The runner’s workspace sandbox is not a per-file allowlist, and its outside-plan and uncommitted findings are informational. If the lane edits an unlisted file, stages a change, or commits, this plan can report rejection after restoring only the recorded bytes. That does not satisfy the prohibition on keeping unaccepted edits. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:118,126-127`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-SPEC.md:403-413,610`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:2173-2182,6277-6296`
- **HIGH — The record can become permanently `running`, and decisions can race.** `O_EXCL` prevents a second claim but makes the file visible before JSON is complete. A failed write, interrupted dispatch, or failed post-run read/publish has no recovery transition. Both decisions then refuse the record forever. Once `ready`, accept and reject can both load the same record and proceed; the runner releases its lock before Python captures the post-pass state, so that lock does not serialize snapshots or decisions. The planted `running` fixture exercises none of those races. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:121,126-127,161,168`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/references/cross-ai.mjs:1658-1663,7162-7169`
- **HIGH — Rollback has incompatible rules for deletion and is not retryable.** Task 1 says to apply the same regular-file check at restore time, while Task 2 requires restoring a deleted file. A partial reject leaves the already-restored row’s old `post_sha256` in the record, so retrying reports that file as an owner change. The record also stores no original mode; restoring a deleted executable with `mkstemp` loses its execute bits. This matters even in a solo checkout. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:121-122,127,163-165`
- **HIGH — The edit pass forks composition and omits the final checks.** It sizes `EDIT_PROMPT` alone, then adds the capability block after claiming state and never checks the final prompt against the window. It also supplies neither the normal MCP selection nor the required soft-confinement text for a `full` lane. With `--cwd` below the git root, it embeds root-relative paths but starts the runner from the subdirectory: `a.txt` can name a different file than the one in the rollback record. Validating only the original input also misses controls in the canonical path that actually goes into the prompt. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:122-128`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:204-205,239`; `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/runner_dispatch.py:531-540`
- **MEDIUM — The unattended task has to invent several decisions.** Task 1’s suggested two-function split leaves the state shape, snapshot/read failure handling, and more than 15 locals in the execute half. Task 2 names 11 tests after Task 1’s two, but requires 14; the ordering and pending-record tests cited in frontmatter and the threat register are not assigned as named behavior tests. Task 3 replaces `DEFAULT_PASS_SEAMS` with a fake while expecting run output, but never specifies how the injected `print_fn` is captured consistently with accept/reject’s direct `print`. It also inherits the make wrapper without scratch `HOME`. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:28,116,126-129,154-176,192-197,235`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:139`; `pyproject.toml:75-80`

##### Suggestions

- Require enforceable edit scope, or isolate the pass and bring back only approved changes. Add a test where the fake lane modifies an unlisted file and the command cannot report a successful rejection while that change remains.
- Specify one recovery protocol that serializes decisions, publishes complete records, exposes interrupted passes, tracks partial restore progress, and preserves original file modes. Reuse the existing atomic byte writer for publication and restore; an exclusive claim is a separate concern. Add tests for interrupted publication, concurrent decisions, retry after partial rejection, and a deleted executable.
- Reuse the final composition and window check from 39-12 with edit-lens selection and a repo-only approved-file policy. Verify the exact paths relative to the actual child cwd. Hand Phase 40 an explicit distinction between **no pass**, **review pending owner decision**, and **finished**; a successful runner exit is not owner acceptance.

##### Risk Assessment

**HIGH.** A normal interruption can strand recovery, and a normal rejection can leave behind changes the owner never accepted. These are release blockers for a command that edits the real checkout.

##### Ponytail findings

- **HIGH — Task 1:** The record and fake-run tracer are necessary, not speculative. The duplicate budget/block pipeline and handwritten atomic publishing are not. Reuse 39-12’s composer and `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/cache.py:27-46`; separate the transaction and recovery tests from the tracer so the task fits the execution cap. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:116-130`
- **HIGH — Task 2:** Acceptance and rollback tests are necessary. Replace the pre-existing-record demonstration with a real overlap test, using the lock-and-update pattern in `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/model_catalog.py:375-388`. Keep the owner-change test, and extend it to a second reject rather than adding another recovery command. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:154-176`
- **MEDIUM — Task 3:** One command with mutually exclusive decisions is enough. Reuse the parser registry and capture pattern in `skills/ai-kit-spec-review/ai_kit_spec/config_status.py:89-102` and `tests/test_config_status.py:44-49`. Pin the actual decision-pending result before the 39-15 documentation and Phase 40 consume it. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:192-200`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md:262-264`

##### Batch checks

- **Wave and dependency ordering:** No same-wave file overlap for this batch. The on-disk plans are the sole occupants of waves 6, 7, and 8, and their `39-11 → 39-12 → 39-13 → 39-14 → 39-15` chain covers the declared helpers and docs handoff. The adapter caller gaps above are missing work, not missing wave edges. `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-12-PLAN.md:5-6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-13-PLAN.md:5-6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-14-PLAN.md:5-6`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-15-PLAN.md:5-6`
- **Coverage still open in this batch:** R8’s missing-list edge and end-to-end embedded-value prohibition; R5’s final-size and read-once edges; R9’s decision concurrency and no-unaccepted-edits prohibition; and R11’s real execute parity. Template/render comparison has a valid existing mechanism: it compares normalized stderr as well as stdout, and 39-02 already invokes it. It does not need a new test harness in this batch. `tests/test_cross_ai_render_sync.py:77-102,260-276`; `.planning/phases/39-lane-capability-blocks-and-lens-skills/39-02-PLAN.md:163`

**Verdict: revise all three before unattended execution.** Read-only source review only: no files changed, and no test suites, lint targets, or live hosts were run. Task timings and runtime behavior remain unmeasured.
